# Changelog

## Beta 1.214 — 2026-11-16

THE NOTE BUTTON MOVES UP (Andrew, 2026-11-16: "Move it up beside the title, like the sample"). On the selection card, the note button leaves the bottom action row and stands in the title row — top-right, beside the street name, vertically centred on the name + status block — the layout of his approved Look 2 sample (the 1.209 round shipped Look 2's colours and type but kept the button in its old row; the sample's simplified card had drawn it beside the title, and Andrew ruled the sample's placement in). THE MARKUP: #selNote moves inside a new title-row wrapper (.selTop, a flex row: the .selTxt text block at flex:1, the button flex:none at its right end) ahead of .selActs; THE CSS: .selTop is the row (align-items centre, 10px gap) and .selTxt takes the row's free width, so the street name ellipsizes against the button — its own nowrap/ellipsis rules — never under it. NOTHING ELSE ABOUT THE BUTTON MOVES: the same 34px ghost circle, the same 16px icon, the same day/night fills (the shipped rule and the sample's .notebtn agree at 34px), the same tap → the same note editor for the street, in every state — renderPanel still drives it by ID (hidden in multi, per Beta 1.31: a multi-select has no one street to note), and no app JS changes at all. THE ACTION ROW: with the button gone, "Complete entire road" (flex:1) spans the row's full inner width in the single state; the multi row (Mark both sides + Clear) is untouched, and .selActs keeps its 42px min-height reserve. THE ONE-SIZE INVARIANT HOLDS EXACTLY: the title text block (~37-38px) is taller than the 34px button, so the title row stands at the block's height with the button inside it — rendered QA on the assembled page (390x844 and 430x932, day + night; single with no note, single with a saved note, multi, and a 46-char stress name) measures the card at 342 x 112.2 in every state, the button's right edge on the card's inner right edge, its centre on the title block's centre, the green button spanning the full inner width, no name/button overlap anywhere (the stress name ellipsizes), the side chips clear of the card, and the note editor opening from the moved button — on Beta 1.213 the position and full-width checks fail (the button sits at the action row's left end) while the size invariant passes, so the QA discriminates the move itself. Proof: tests/proof/proof-selcard-note-move.mjs (new, wired into build-and-test; fails on Beta 1.213, passes here) pins the structure (the button inside .selTop ahead of .selActs, .selActs free of it), the row CSS, the button's unchanged 34px rule, the .selActs reserve, renderPanel's by-ID contract, and the bump's agreement; drive128 gains the M13 rendered checks (title-row position, full-width action button, stress-name clearance — the same properties, in the gate's own drive), and drive194's session version pin plus the two earlier proofs' version pins roll to Beta 1.214 in place (TEST class, the standing pattern). Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.213 (main at c2c246f0) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.214 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.214; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.213.

## Beta 1.213 — 2026-11-16

THE NEW ICON, EVERYWHERE (Andrew, 2026-11-16: "new icon everywhere"). Andrew's professional icon pack replaces the Beta 1.177 icon set whole — he replaced the source pack mid-round (a clay corner-fix pack was built first and discarded whole before it shipped; this entry describes only what lands). Every file is a byte-for-byte copy of the pack's, sha256-pinned in the proof: icon-192, icon-512, icon-1024, apple-touch-icon, icon-32, icon-16 — the with-background files wear the pack's designed blue gradient field, opaque in every corner (no white, no transparency — the corner defect the swap exists to fix). THE MASKABLE IS THE PACK'S OWN COMPOSITION, copied direct: a flat (27,47,182) field with the artwork at bbox (64,127)–(447,447) on the summed-diff instrument, inside the 80% safe-zone square [51..461] on every side; rendered mask QA (circle + squircle overlays, in the round record) shows the squircle cropping nothing and a strict circle cropping only the snow band's bottom corners — the truck, blade and lights sit inside the circle whole. THE SPLASH ARTWORK is the pack's transparent foreground rendering (web/icons/foreground-transparent-512.png), already 512×512 as shipped, replacing the 512×423 file; scripts/assemble.py inlines it by the unchanged mechanism, and the splash design is untouched (artwork alone on the gradient, no tile, no radius, no shadow — Beta 1.191). The taller box (168×168 at the tile-era 168px width, was 168×138.8) moves drive180 pins, all TEST class, each re-derived from the new asset's own measurements at the same strength: ICONSIZE (the rendered box wears the asset's aspect — now square — within 2px and never changes size in the hold); NOTILE1 (natural size 512×512; the no-tile probes move to the points this artwork actually leaves transparent — both top corners, top-mid, left-mid and right-mid, all measured alpha 0 — because this artwork's snow band runs opaque across the full bottom edge by design, so the bottom corners are pinned AS artwork (alpha 255): the asset's shape is asserted, not assumed, and a tiled asset still fails every clear probe); NOTILE2 (code unchanged — it samples 12%/12% of the box wherever the box is; its name text now cites the square asset's transparent point (61,61), measured alpha 0). CENTRE/HOLD1/SEQ/S8/CUT stand unmodified — the brand layer centres the taller box exactly as it centred the shorter one. FAVICON: favicon.svg is REPLACED with the pack's master vector (vectors/master-full-square.svg — a true vector, zero embedded rasters per the pack's own QA report and verified here, rendered standalone in QA); the existing SVG head link stays and now serves the new icon, with the PNG 32/16 links behind it. drive194's session version pin rolls to Beta 1.213 with the label (TEST class). Proof: tests/proof/proof-icon-swap-1213.mjs (new, wired into build-and-test; fails on Beta 1.212, passes here) — pack-byte hashes for all nine files, opaque corners on the with-background set, the maskable's flat field and safe-zone bbox, the splash's size and transparency points, the favicon's identity with the pack master, the bump's agreement. THE SERVICE WORKER DOES NOT CHANGE: it carries no icon bytes and no icon URLs, and iOS keeps the home-screen icon frozen at install regardless (delete/re-add, Backup export first — the standing Beta 1.171 note). NOTHING ELSE MOVES: no app logic, no behaviour, no other asset. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.212 (main at 9f9fb6e2) exactly, the old icon set restored whole. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.213 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.213; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.212.

## Beta 1.212 — 2026-11-16

THE WORDING RULE NOW COVERS FINISHED CLEANS TOO (Andrew, 2026-11-16: "Change those two as well"). Beta 1.211's sweep changed every user-facing string that called the IN-PROGRESS clean a "run" and listed two it left because their "run" names a finished clean; Andrew ruled the rule covers finished cleans too, so both now say "clean": the plain-text export's per-archive line 'Archived run (finished ...)' -> 'Archived clean (finished ...)', and the saved-clean detail's gap line '...measured from the previous completion, or the run start' -> 'or the clean start'. The census was re-run over every quoted string literal in the parts (the 1.211 method) and agrees with the 1.211 census: no other finished-clean "run" exists — the remaining hits name something else (a download "already running", the app component that "is not running", a street that "runs past the prepared area" — the geometry sense) and stay. Internal identifiers, storage keys, comments and release history are untouched; no behaviour changes. Proof: tests/proof/proof-wording-finished-cleans.mjs (new, wired into build-and-test; fails on Beta 1.211, passes here). The drive194 session version pin rolls to Beta 1.212 with the label (TEST class — the pin follows the shipped version). Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.211 (main at 13d003d) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.212 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.212; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.211.

## Beta 1.211 — 2026-11-15

THREE APPROVED CHANGES (a fourth approved scope closed on its census — see the end). (1) THE CREATE-FLOW TRIM GLOW IS A SHADER NOW: Andrew approved the real shader sample on 2026-11-15 ("I like the way it looks. Let's do it."), and the create map's sector-trim glow — through 1.210 a five-layer blur stack (crGlow1-5 over a GeoJSON source, widths riding 1.210's crZoomW px curve) — is now ONE custom WebGL layer, crGlowShader, porting the sample's measured construction (work/glow-shader-sample/RECORD.md): a ribbon mesh built in METRES around the glow's lines with NINE cross-section rings at -20/-13/-9.9/-4.95/0/+4.95/+9.9/+13/+20 m carrying a per-vertex distance attribute (a two-row ribbon interpolates the distance to the outer value everywhere and shades to nothing — the sample round's load-bearing lesson; the two outer ring pairs carry the low-zoom floor's tail below and are alpha-0 at the picking zooms), the fragment shader holding full strength inside the road's kerb (4.95 m, the engine's SURF_HALF) and decaying (1-t)^2 across a breathing reach — 4.0 s cycle, reach 5<->9 m, peak alpha 0.50<->0.80, in phase — with ONE measured deviation from the sample's absolute form, a reach floor: the create casing balloons in metres toward 1.210's z15 anchor (22 m wide there, holding those metres below z15), which swallows a purely metric halo whole at z15 and below (this round's rendered QA measured it: no visible glow at z13, the fringe ending inside the casing at z15), so the reach takes a floor sized to hold 30% of peak alpha at the casing's edge — (casingHalfM - 4.95) x 2.21, casingHalfM being the casing's metre half-width at max(z, 15) — which restores a real fringe past the paint and a warm tint through the 55%-opaque casing at z15/z13 while leaving z16+ (casing half 5.5 m or less, floor <= 1.2, under the breath's own trough) running the approved sample form untouched — miter joins clamped at cos 0.45 with a segment-normal hairpin fallback, round end-cap fans, premultiplied "over" blending (ONE / ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA) so crossings saturate at the class colour and never blow out, depth test and depth write off, and the GL state snapshotted and restored around the draw — the GPS puck layer's discipline (Beta 1.188). Because the mesh is metric, the glow scales with the map like real paint at every zoom (the property crZoomW gave the stack by hand; the curve itself still drives the draft casing and lines, untouched). The breathing repaint runs only while the mesh is non-empty — an empty glow draws nothing and requests no frames. syncCrGlow is still the glow's ONE writer with its 1.209 chosen-sectors-only feature computation byte-identical; only its sink changed (source.setData -> crGlowSetFeatures, which rebuilds the mesh). The main map's clean-time selection glow is a different stack in the engine and is untouched, as is every other create-flow behaviour. (2) THE WORDING SWEEP — A CLEAN IS NOT A RUN (Andrew: "we're not running"): every user-facing string that called the in-progress clean a "run" now says "clean": the Pace header 'This run' -> 'Current clean'; the Pace empty line 'when you finish a run and tap Finish' -> 'a clean'; the start warning 'the run state could not be saved' -> 'the clean state'; the cancel toasts 'Start cancelled — run erased' -> 'clean erased' (x3), 'rebuilt from the marks made during the run' -> 'during the clean' (x3 in app3, x2 in app4 — the restore and resume toasts), 'the run may return on reload' -> 'the clean may return'; the finish storage toast 'finish stopped, run intact' -> 'clean intact'; the resume toast 'Resumed run —' -> 'Resumed clean —'. Census method: every quoted string literal in the parts scanned for a standalone "run", each hit hand-classified; internal identifiers (runStart, runDist, storage keys), comments and geometry "runs" are untouched by design, and two strings were listed and LEFT because their "run" names a finished clean, not the one in progress: the plain-text export's 'Archived run (finished ...)' line and the saved-clean detail's 'or the run start' — both flagged to Andrew with this release. (3) TWO TAB ICONS, DRAWN PROPERLY (Andrew's picks from the icon sample): Streets wears the straight-on road — a straight road slab with three centre dashes, ONE evenodd path (the sample's own path) replacing the Beta 1.28 perspective road; Settings wears the spoked cog from Andrew's photo — a ring of 12 teeth, three spokes meeting at a centre hub — authored as ONE nonzero path (18 subpaths: 12 teeth, ring outer wound with the silhouette, ring inner wound against it for the hole, 3 spokes, hub), the spokes overlapping the ring band and the hub so the silhouette is connected BY CONSTRUCTION (Andrew caught a spoke gap in the sample; the shipped path cannot have one — proven by a pixel walk along each spoke in the round's QA, and the path renders pixel-equal to the sample composite, filled-pixel ratio 1.0002). Both stay in the pack's frame (viewBox 0 0 24 24, fill currentColor) and render on every surface the entries feed. SCOPE CLOSED ON ITS CENSUS — THE ROUTE POI PINS KEEP THEIR RENDERING: the round's fourth approved scope asked for the routepoi pins (main-map route marker + create-flow route pins) to be re-drawn shader-style "for quality". The census found all four pin layers are MapLibre circle layers — GPU signed-distance-field renders, resolution-independent by construction, anti-aliased in the fragment shader — with NO raster, sprite or image asset anywhere in the pin path, and the pin is flat (a white ring, a solid dot): there is no quality gap for a shader to close, and a hand-written shader replica of a flat SDF circle is pixel-equivalent. Per the round brief's own stop rule the scope closed with no code change; the full census, the environment limit that kept a local edge transect from being taken (below z~13 the headless VM stalls all engine paint while basemap tiles fail through the proxy — the POI's whole visible band sits inside that stall; the street paint Andrew uses daily stalls identically), and one observation (the pins lie in the ground plane at pitch — circle-pitch-alignment unset — a shape choice, not a resolution defect) are recorded in the round record. Proof: tests/proof/proof-shader-glow-round.mjs (new, wired into build-and-test; 32 checks — the mesh builder runs against a Mercator stub: vertex counts, the exact ring distance set {0, 4.95, 9.9, 13, 20}, feature colours, the 20 m metric reach within the Float32Array ULP; the shader's falloff/breath/blend constants and the reach floor; the wording before/after sets; both icon paths' shape — 22 checks fail on 1.210). Rendered QA (this round's record): z17 transect smooth (kerb plateau even, monotone (1-t)^2 decay, no shelf or cliff, background by 15.5 m), breathing phase-locked across four frames (width 11.2<->16.4 m, rank correlation 1.00), picked-sectors-only exact (removed sector's halo measures 0), crossings saturate gold without whitening (corner B/R 0.29), z15/z13 presence past the ballooned casing, night plateau on its phase prediction, both icons crisp at tab size day + night. Three existing proofs re-derived at the same strength where they had pinned the retired stack (TEST class, dated notes in each): proof-trim-glow-path-pills S1 (the sink is now the mesh builder's feature capture; every feature-set check unchanged), proof-green-half-road R7, proof-create-zoom-tapaway S1 (the glow's crZoomW entries retire with the stack). Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.210 (main at 09cfe7f5) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.211 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.211; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.210.

## Beta 1.210 — 2026-11-15

TWO CREATE-A-ROUTE FIXES FROM ANDREW'S BETA 1.209 PHONE SESSION. (1) THE SELECTION PAINT STAYS PROPORTIONATE TO THE ZOOM (his headline ask, stated twice): the create map's draft paint — the casing under chosen sectors (12.75 px), the draft line itself (6.75 px chosen, 4.5 px unchosen) and the sector-trim glow stack — wore CONSTANT pixel widths tuned at z17 (Beta 1.182), so its drawn width in metres doubled with every zoom level down: the casing draws ≈5.5 m at z17 but ≈22 m at z15, ≈88 m at z13 and ≈352 m at z11, and on his zoomed-out screen nearby picks melted into a single pink/gold blob (his Vellore screenshot). Every one of those widths now rides ONE curve (crZoomW in create-pick.js): the anchor value holds at z15 and above — the picking zooms are pixel-identical to 1.209, and the proof pins the drawn width in metres at z15/z17 unchanged — and the px width halves per level below z15, so the drawn width holds its z15 metre value all the way down the range (the same constant-metres principle the main map's surfW/surfCASE curves follow). At z12 the casing draws ≈1.6 px against thread-width roads: separate lines, no blob. The draft line draws as TWO layers now (crDraftLine chosen-only, crDraftLineDim unchosen-only, same colours and widths as the single layer's branches) because MapLibre silently drops a layer whose line-width nests a zoom curve inside the chosen switch — the layer never enters the style and the paint never renders, with no error thrown; the round's rendered QA caught the coloured line missing entirely on the first build and the proof now bans the shape. Rendered QA (screenshot transects over three seeded parallel streets 30–45 m apart, day + night): on 1.209 the painted extent measures 22.4 m at z15 but 165 m at z13 and 248 m at z12 with the pair merged into one run; on 1.210 it holds 22.4 m → 24.1 m → 27.6 m with a clear unpainted gap at both far zooms, and the z17 extent is pixel-identical to z15 (13 px) on both builds. The glow's stack table keeps its anchor literals (the 1.208/1.209 probes and the green-half-road proof pins survive untouched) and its blur stays constant, as the main map's glow blur does. NOT SCALED, DELIBERATELY: crDraftHit, the invisible 24 px sector tap target — it is a tap target, not paint, and street picking resolves through its own pixel query boxes (CR_TAP_BOXES) against the basemap, so picking keeps its exact reach at every zoom and cannot get worse; the search POI markers are screen-size markers by design and are likewise untouched. The main map's paint is not in this release at all (no engine change). (2) TAP-AWAY LEAVES THE SECTOR TRIM, AS CANCEL: the trim view (tap a picked road again; glow on, "Tap sectors to add or remove them") could be exited without saving only via its Cancel button. A map tap during a trim that resolves to NO sector of the trim street — beyond the rendered-feature boxes and beyond the geometry fallback's reach (20 px / 75 m, the standing 1.156 tap discipline) — now runs the whole existing crCancelSectors path: the entry snapshot is restored (a sector removed during the session is picked again), the session closes and the glow goes out. crSectorTap learned to answer whether it toggled (its resolution logic is byte-identical; only the return contract is new), and crMapTap's session branch routes a miss to crCancelSectors — no parallel exit was built. Sector taps still toggle and never exit; taps on the trim card, the search bar and the other create chrome are DOM and never reach the map; and a drag or pan can never cancel a trim, because MapLibre's click — the only event crMapTap consumes — fires only when press and release land within its click tolerance, the same tap-vs-drag discipline the main map's tap-away deselect already rests on (Andrew's standing menu-consistency rule: tap-away works like everywhere else). Entire road, Done and the Cancel button itself are unchanged. Proof: tests/proof/proof-create-zoom-tapaway.mjs (new, wired into build-and-test; evaluates the shipped curve — px pinned at z15/17/19, metres constant below z15 — and runs crSectorTap's real resolution in a harness: on-sector tap toggles and answers true, 30 m and 300 m misses answer false; 8 checks fail on 1.209). Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.209 (main at 75d1f49f) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.210 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.210; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.209.

## Beta 1.209 — 2026-11-15

FIVE FIXES FROM ANDREW'S BETA 1.208 PHONE SESSION (items 4-5 added the same evening, before the gate closed). (1) SIDE ARROWS FOLLOW YOUR DRIVE: multi-selecting sections and tapping a side chip applied ONE stored side number to every selected stretch — but a stored side is numbered against its own street's drawn direction, so when the selection turned a corner (his drive: sections along one primary, then a turn onto the next), one tap lit his right side on one street and his left side on the other. The press now follows the drive: the tapped sections in tap order chain into drives (consecutive taps join when an end of one sits within 20 m of an end of the next — measured on Route 5W's real stretch data, true junction joins are exact shared vertices at 0 m and the nearest non-joining part ends on the route are 58.7 m apart, so the tolerance sits in a wide empty band); each section's travel direction comes from its drive neighbours; the press's meaning (driver's right or left) is read off the anchor section — the last tapped, where the chips sit — and mapped per section through its own travel-vs-drawn direction (new pure helper groupSideMap in app2.js, consumed by both the press, togglePieceGroup, and the chips' done state, groupSideDone, so a chip reads the same side its press writes). Sections that don't chain keep the old raw mapping exactly, as do lone sections and a press whose anchor section stands alone; "Mark both sides" is unchanged; no stored data changes — only which bit a press sets. (2) THE TRIM GLOW COVERS ONLY THE SECTORS STILL PICKED: creating a route, the sector-trim glow (Beta 1.208) carried the street's whole line, so a sector tapped OFF turned grey but kept glowing. syncCrGlow now builds the glow from the same sector record the draft paint uses (crStreetParts + crChosenSet): a removed sector's grey carries no glow, re-adding a sector restores its glow, and every toggle re-renders the draft so the glow tracks each tap; closing the trim still puts the glow out entirely. The grey removed-sector paint is untouched. (3) THE CLASS PILLS WEAR TRUE BLACK: the 1.208 dark ink (#4a3200, Andrew's pick C at the time) read faded and muddy on the raspberry pill to his eye; shown ink variants he picked TRUE BLACK — .crChip and .crPChip, PRIMARY and SECONDARY, now read #000000. Letter case stays ALL CAPS (shown both, he kept caps); no pill size, shape or spacing changes. The PENDING chip (white on amber) is a different state and stays. (4) THE SELECTION CARD WEARS LOOK 2, AND ITS BUTTON SAYS WHAT IT DOES: Andrew found the card loud and its button misleading — "Mark completed" reads as finishing only the lit stretch, but the action completes the WHOLE road. The card now wears the Look 2 sample he approved, in both themes: a solid card (white by day, near-solid charcoal rgba(20,21,27,.97) at night), hairline border, 16px radius, 13/14 padding, one flat soft shadow — the glass/blur and the 1.32 street-colour left edge are retired — title 16px/800, sub-line 12px in the flat theme greys, the note button a 34px ghost circle with a 16px icon, and the action button a 42px mint-tint slab (day rgba(6,180,130,.13) with #087a5b text; night rgba(6,214,160,.15) with #2fe0ae) that Mark both sides shares, so the two still wear the one official green (drive128 M11, re-derived to the tint). The button reads "Complete entire road"; "Mark not done" is unchanged, in the same tint form in red; multi's "Mark both sides" and "Clear" labels are untouched (Clear takes the row's 42px height as a ghost). The census for the retired label found exactly two surfaces — renderPanel's button text and the resting markup — both changed; the action itself (markStreet) is untouched. The note editor does not live in this card (it is its own modal) and is untouched. The Beta 1.34 one-size rule holds: the action row reserves 42px in both states. drive128 M7/M10/M11, drive125 P4 and the E-06 proof's note-height pin were re-derived at the same strength (TEST-class) where they had pinned the retired edge or the solid green. (5) THE PROGRESS PILL'S SPACE IS ALWAYS RESERVED — THE DOCK NEVER MOVES: Beta 1.202 made the pill appear only during a clean and its delivery flagged that the dock compacted by the pill's 37px footprint, leaving the Start circle 37px lower when idle; Andrew hit the jump live and ruled the Start circle and the flanking buttons stay in the position they occupy with the pill present. updateCount now toggles the pill's VISIBILITY (its resting markup state likewise): the 1.202 visibility rule is unchanged (Start → shown, Finish/Cancel → hidden, boot mid-run → shown, marking outside a clean still counts internally), but the pill's slot is permanently reserved, so it appears into its spot and nothing moves — measured pixel-identical (circle, both flanking buttons, dock bottom) between idle and running at both phone widths, day and night. drive198 was re-derived at the same strength to the stronger property (its P4 now asserts NO movement; P0/P1/P6 read the reserved-slot states). Proofs: tests/proof/proof-trim-glow-path-pills.mjs (new; the side-arrow cases run on Route 5W's real stretch data — Andrew's Valeria/Greenpark turn, a straight Fifth→Angie continuation, a left turn, unconnected sections, a single-street regression, out-of-order taps and one street driven in opposite directions — and fail on 1.208; S4/S5 sections pin the card's Look 2 values, the retired label's absence, and the visibility toggle). Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.208 (main at 7920dabe) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.209 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.209; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.208.

## Beta 1.208 — 2026-11-15

THE GREEN PAINT MATCHES THE STREET (Andrew, from his Beta 1.206 phone screenshots on Valeria Boulevard). (1) FULL GREEN IS THE SAME WIDTH AS THE OTHER STREETS: a both-sides-done street drew its green on the wider BANDW curve from 1.206 (~14.4 m against the surface's 9.9 m); the band layers (t1-band, t1-selband, the selection glow/core '-b' variants) now draw with surfW itself, both casings share the one surfCASE curve, BANDW/BANDCASE/BAND_HALF are retired, and band end trims use SURF_HALF — so a completed street's green is exactly as wide as a not-done street at every zoom (rendered, measured: width ratio 1.00 at z18/19 against 1.45 shipped), and its ends meet crossing paint at the same 4.95 m edge as every surface end (the 1.206 street-end fix does not regress — re-measured at Kingsnorth × Santa Barbara, identical or better at every end). (2) ONE SIDE DONE FILLS HALF THE ROAD: a single done side drew as a fixed 4.5 px line on the kerb offset — half the road zoomed out, a thin kerb outline zoomed in (measured 9 device px wide at z17, z18 and z19 alike). The engine now builds a FILL family on half-offset lines (±2.475 m, centreline to kerb), drawn at half the surface width (surfW × 0.5): the done side reads half green / half street-colour at every zoom (pixel transects: green spans centreline to kerb, 0.48–0.52 of the road's drawn width at z16–19, day and night). The old t1-edge layer is retired (its edges geometry still builds, undrawn, as the audit surface). (3) SELECTING A STRETCH NO LONGER BREAKS ITS GREEN: the selection rebuilt the stretch's side line from that one piece alone, re-clipping the junction ends the board line runs through (measured on Andrew's exact spot, Valeria stretch 10 at Romeo Crescent: the board's corner point sat 5.70 m off the selection's line — the visible disconnect). The selection's side paint (new selFill source / t1-selfill layer) is now built by the same fillSlicePts helper as the board's fills, so it is a contiguous sub-path of the board strand by construction — selected and deselected paint are the same points (verified exactly, 21 of 21 points). (4) STREET NAMES NO LONGER REPEAT ALONG THE STREET: zoomed in, "Valeria Boulevard" repeated 4–5 times per screen — the basemap clone layers' line-placed labels repeating at the renderer's default 250 px. Route streets are now named by the app's own titles at EVERY zoom: the title layers (labelpts — exactly one name per street, at the street's anchor) no longer end at 16.4 (no maxzoom, no fade-out; the size ramp gains a z19 stop so names stay readable), the basemap clones are retired (never built; removed if an older build left one), and the originals keep excluding route names — so a route street's name can print at most once on any screen, the way the zoomed-out view Andrew likes already worked. (The first build of this item widened the clones' label repeat toward ~500 m instead; the gate measured that cure dead in this renderer — a 300 px repeat already loses Malden Street's label and 400 px loses every label in the test view, because line-label anchors earn their place per tile segment — so the mechanism changed, not the rule. Beta 1.40's handover, titles-out/basemap-in across 15.4–16.4, is retired with the clones.) RUN-THROUGH HELD: on the continuity round's census (53 junction points) the new green has gap 0 at every junction in every board state (one-side, both-sides, checker, permix), including with a stretch selected; centres/hit/labelpts geometry is unchanged in every uniform state, and every other geometry difference is an end trim beside a re-trimmed band (enumerated in the round's RECORD). Tap tolerance follows the widest paint as before, now the surface casing: 24 px to z17, 27.5 px at z18, 50.8 px at z19 (was 39/73.1 against the retired band casing). Proofs: tests/proof/proof-green-half-road.mjs (new; fails 12 checks on 1.206) and the street-end proof updated to the superseded band spec (its checks 3/5/6/8 re-based; the street-end property checks are unchanged). This release SUPERSEDES Beta 1.206's wider completed band. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.207 (main at 51ad0e17) exactly. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.208 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.208; no maintenance PRs since Beta 1.207.

SAME RELEASE, CREATE-FLOW FOLLOW-UPS (Andrew, same session). (5) BOTH CLASS PILLS WEAR THE SAME DARK INK: the SECONDARY pill's white text was chosen for Andrew in 1.207, not by him; shown the three variants in a sample, he picked C — both dark — so .crChip.sec and .crPChip.sec now read #4a3200 on the raspberry, exactly the ink PRIMARY wears on the gold (the PRIMARY rules are untouched; the PENDING chip's white on amber is a different state and stays). (6) "TAP ROW TO ADJUST" STANDS ON ITS OWN LINE: a picked road's panel row read "Entire road · 318 m — tap row to adjust" on one line (or the "N of M sectors" variant); the instruction is now its own line under the length line, same words, in the one row renderer that prints it (crPanelRowEl — the preview list's rows never carried the subtitle). (7) THE STREET BEING TRIMMED GLOWS ON THE CREATE MAP: opening a street's sector trim now lights that street with the main map's selection glow, mirrored onto the create map's own draft layers (five halo layers in the street's category colour under its draft paint, the surface width's +30/+23/+17/+9/+4 offsets on the draft's scale, blur on the outer pair) for exactly as long as the trim view is open — every close path (Done, Cancel, stage change, street removal, creation exit) puts it out, and the street's normal draft paint is untouched underneath. GATE FIX (Codex P2, in-round): the generic junction end-clip solves its cut along the street's centreline, which dragged a terminating fill's end sideways off its half-offset line (0.61 m from the centreline instead of 2.475 m — green spilling onto the unfinished side); fill ends now clip ON their own line (clipOnLine: the generic solve's cap position, projected back onto the half-offset line), proven on the proof network's new stopping case and on Route 5W itself — every fill endpoint in every board state now sits within its 2.475 m band (was: most junction ends pulled to 1.0–1.6 m), with the run-through still gap 0 at all 53 junctions and centres/bands/edges geometry byte-identical to the pre-fix build. GATE FIX 2 (Codex P2, gate round 2): the fills had no corner treatment - where one street's marked side turns a corner into another street's marked side, the two fills stopped at the crossing street's near edge and the green pinched across the junction square instead of wrapping the corner (the retired kerb-line paint traced the kerb around corners). fillCornerLinks now adds a connector strand through the two half lines' own intersection wherever two fill ends face each other around a corner at a junction (14 connectors on the checker board; uniform boards need none - a through street's fill already runs through, and a terminating fill meets the through fill's edge full-width). A lone side still ends at the near edge, exactly as a lone surface does. GATE FIX 3 (two Codex P2s, gate round 3): (a) FILL-TO-BAND CONTINUITY - a one-side piece flowing into a both-sides piece is ONE completed side, but the fill clipped at the family boundary while the band clipped at its own, leaving the junction square's half (9.9 m) unpainted on the proof network (LongAve [1, 3, 3]). The clip rule is now per-side-paint: a fill keeps its shared vertex beside a band, a band keeps its vertex beside any marked piece (and the strand-end pass skips band ends whose green continues as a fill; the selection's band redraw follows the same rule), so the strands meet at the boundary in both directions. (b) OBLIQUE JUNCTION CAPS - the gate-round-2 clipOnLine PROJECTED the solved end pair onto the half-offset line, exact at perpendicular crossings but not at oblique ones: at a 45° crossing the cap's centre sat ~6.2 m from the crossing centreline instead of on the 4.95 m paint edge, leaving a ~3 m wedge. The pair now moves by INTERSECTION - the solved end travels along the crossing edge's own line to where it meets the half-offset line, the approach keeping its edge-normal shape - so both cap corners lie on the crossing edge at any angle; a validity window (near the solved end, near the line) guards bent skewed T-junctions, where the projection remains as the bounded fallback. On Route 5W's own geometry the round changes fill ends at skewed junctions only: bands and centres are byte-identical to the previous build, the run-through is still gap 0 at all 53 junctions in every state, the new side-strip continuity measurement (samples every metre along every continuing side's half line) is clean in every state, and every fill endpoint sits in its 2.475 m band (one at 3.21 m by the finite-strand ruler at the Dianawood x Wilshire skewed T - on its half line, with both centre strands clipped short of the corner). GATE FIX 4 (one Codex P2 + the label drive's evidence, gate round 4): (a) CORNER CONNECTORS RIDE THE SELECTION - selecting a one-side piece at a wrapped corner redrew only the piece's own fill slice, and the selected surface painted over the board's corner connector, reopening a ~5 m break for exactly as long as the stretch stayed selected (Codex's proof-network case: LongAve [2, 0, 0] x CrossSt [0, 2, 0]). buildSelFill now also redraws any board corner connector touching a selected side, from the board's own strands, so selected and deselected paint identically there too (proof R3b). (b) THE LABEL RULE CHANGED MECHANISM, per item (4) above: drive130's L7 failed on three consecutive gate heads with empty label sets while passing on 1.207 - the widened label repeat was killing the names it was meant to space out. drive130 is rewritten to the shipped rule (titles print the route name exactly once at close zoom; the basemap prints none; the clones never exist), drive131's constructor check follows it, and drive156's row check was updated to R6's split subtitle (its two-line form) - no check weakened; each now pins the new contract at the same strength. GATE FIX 5 (one Codex P2, gate round 4 continued): the connector redraw of GATE FIX 4 identified connectors by mem count, but an ordinary fill strand merged from two consecutive pieces carries two mem entries too - selecting one of two same-side pieces redrew the whole strand and painted the unselected piece in selection green (proof network: to x ~= 55.47 past the boundary). Connectors now carry an explicit flag set where fillCornerLinks creates them, and buildSelFill copies only flagged strands (proof R3c pins both directions: the corner connector rides, the ordinary two-piece strand does not).

## Beta 1.207 — 2026-11-15

CREATING A ROUTE IS CLEANER (Andrew, from his 1.205 device session): five tidy-ups in and around the create-a-route flow, one design language throughout. (1) THE CLASS PILLS MATCH NOW: in the pick list the secondary chip was a pale outlined pill reading "2ND" and in the review list a plain grey one, while PRIMARY wore a filled gold pill — now BOTH steps wear the one filled pill: PRIMARY in yellow (unchanged), SECONDARY spelled out in full, filled in the secondary red (#ef476f, white text), same size, shape and type in the pick list (.crPChip) and the review list (.crChip). Tapping a pill still flips the street's class, exactly as before. (2) THE CREATE MENU IS TITLES ONLY: the three doors lost their description lines and read "Select from list", "Manual search" (was "Search manually") and "Upload photo" (was "From a photo" — the photo pane's title matches, and the city library's failure note names Manual search too). The doors are one shared pane, so every entry (cold start, Routes menu, the in-flow gate) shows the same three titles with no leftover space where the descriptions were. (3) THE CITY LIST IS NAMES ONLY: each city row drops its "N streets · ready to load" line; the gold "· last used" tag stays, trailing the city name on the last-used city's row (it rode the removed line before). The loading row ("Loading cities…") and the manual-search results (whose second line is the rest of the address) are unchanged. (4) THE "CURRENTLY LOADED" BADGE SITS BESIDE THE ROUTE NAME: on the Choose-a-route list the white badge rode the row's right edge while the name centred across the full row, so the pill sat ON the name's end (Andrew's screenshot: it covered the tail of "Route 5W"); the badge now lives inside the name's own centred line, name and badge as one group — a long name shrinks and clamps inside the group instead of sliding under the badge (the group is capped at its column, so a loaded created route's badge also stays clear of its Delete button — a gate review catch, measured). (5) THE SAVE POP-UP IS SHORT: after saving, the toast said "Route saved — its maps are not downloaded yet; get them in Settings → Status → Download"; it now says exactly "Route saved", fading as before (the failure messages are unchanged). ## Beta 1.206 — 2026-11-15

STREET ENDS MEET THE CROSSING PAINT WHEN ZOOMED IN (Andrew's report: the coloured streets — gold primaries, raspberry secondaries — "are not connecting at the end of streets" at zoomed-in levels, though zoomed out they looked fine): the ends were never cut wrong. Every terminating end on Route 5W is cut flush to the crossing street's paint edge (clipEndAtJunction: the end cap sits exactly SURF_HALF, 4.95 m, from the crossing centreline — the designed edge of the 9.9 m surface; measured on all 53 junction ends, 4.89–4.95 m, and all 15 dead ends reach their true endpoints exactly). The defect was the DRAWN width: the paint width curves were hand-tuned in screen pixels and, on the app's true map scale (9.24 px/m at z19, measured from the live map's own projection), the surface drew at only 8.4 m at z16, 6.2 m at z17, 4.9 m at z18 and 3.9 m at z19 against its 9.9 m geographic width — so the crossing street's paint as drawn stopped up to ~3 m short of the edge every end was cut to meet, and each end hung over bare asphalt (~1.9 m of gap at z17, ~2.5 m at z18, ~3.0 m at z19; none zoomed out, where the drawn width meets or exceeds the design — exactly Andrew's "fine zoomed out, gapped zoomed in"). Completed streets' bands carried the same family of defect (flush cut at BAND_HALF 7.2 m against a 14.4 m band drawn at 7.1 m at z17, and the BANDW z15 stop drawing only 7.8 m). THE FIX IS WIDTH STOPS ONLY — the z16–19 stops of surfW and the t1-casing curve now hold the drawn surface at the geographic 9.9 m, BANDW holds the drawn band at 14.4 m from z15 up, and BANDCASE keeps the shipped casing rim over BANDW at every stop; the z14 surface stop is untouched (Andrew's approved zoomed-out look), and the selection glow derives from surfW/BANDW and follows on its own. ONE VALUE WAS RULED ON, NOT COPIED: the investigation candidate's BANDCASE z15 stop (10.8) had no derivation and contradicted the investigation record's own spec ("BANDW + the same rim as before at every stop" — it would have fattened the z15 band casing rim from 2.5 to 5.26 spec); the shipped value is 8.04, which keeps the shipped 2.5 rim over the corrected BANDW z15 exactly, and the proof test pins it. ZERO GEOMETRY CHANGE, PROVEN: the mergeStrands-protocol diff (canonical hashes of every painted source — centres, bands, edges, hit — across board states zero, three, one, velmar0, velmar1 and greenpark0) is EMPTY against the base, so the marked side lines' run-through at junctions (Andrew's standing paint rule) is byte-identical to before, and no paint endpoint moves anywhere. DESIGN QA IN THE RENDERED APP: live paint-spec read-backs at z14–19 (drawn surface 9.86–9.87 m at z16–19 against the shipped 8.41/6.14/4.85/3.88; drawn band 14.3–14.4 m at z15–19 against the shipped 7.8/7.1; casing rims equal to shipped at every stop; the z14 surface stop identical at 3.75 px), and screenshots at Kingsnorth × Santa Barbara and Greenpark × Valeria (z17/18/19, day and night) show the terminating paint running into the crossing paint where the shipped build shows the bare gap. The pinning proof (tests/proof/proof-street-end-width-fix.mjs, wired into the build-and-test job) evaluates the four shipped curves as the app builds them: all checks pass with the fix, 16 fail on the unmodified base. THE GATE CAUGHT A COMPANION DEFECT (Codex P2 on this PR, real, fixed in-round): the map-tap tolerance was a fixed 24 px — the Beta 1.181 value, chosen so "a tap anywhere on the paint still selects instead of deselecting" — and the widened paint outgrows it at z18–19 (band + casing half-width 37 / 71 px), so a tap on the outer paint of a street at close zoom would have deselected instead of selecting. The tolerance now lives in the engine (GLP.tapTolPx) and follows the widest paint at the tap's zoom, floored at the same 24 px — at z17 and below the paint is narrower than the floor, so low-zoom tapping is byte-for-byte the shipped behaviour; the proof pins the formula and its coverage at every zoom. NOT IN THIS RELEASE: Topper × Mellings keeps a true ~2 m corner shortfall in its geometry (the one genuine geometric miss the investigation found; the wider crossing paint covers most of it visually) — a fix there is a separate, junction-geometry change and was deliberately not bundled. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.205 + the two maintenance merges (main at 8343246d) exactly. plus 2 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.205 (PR #78, the GLP.init stale-state fix; PR #79, the denylist B-02/A-05 fixes). ## Beta 1.205 — 2026-11-15

THE GOLD ROADS ARE CALLED PRIMARIES NOW (Andrew: "my roads that I clean — there's primaries and there's secondaries; primaries are the yellow ones"): every place the app called the gold roads "Mains" says "Primaries" instead — the Streets menu's two tabs and its group heading, Pace's Progress-by-group bar, and the whole create-a-route flow (the pick rows' PRIMARY/2ND chips, the preview and save count lines, which now read "N primaries"). The word is renamed through the code as well, so it has one name everywhere: the live set is PRIMARIES, the Streets filter value is 'primaries', the create draft's set is draft.primaries, the draft layer's feature property and the gold rows' CSS class are 'primary'. ONE NAME DELIBERATELY DOES NOT CHANGE: the pack field `main` — the list of primary street names inside every route pack — is stored user data (created routes live in the on-device user-pack store and ride backups as userpacks; the city packs download in the same shape), so the stored form stays byte-compatible and every pack, backup and download written by any older build keeps loading exactly as before; the pack validator also keeps accepting the legacy `mains` input key beside the new `primaries` key. Nothing about the roads themselves, their order, their colours or any saved data changes — same roads, new name. ## Beta 1.204 — 2026-11-15

THE PUCK SITS ON THE ROAD (Andrew, after judging the replay sample 2026-11-14: "Let's snap it"): driving, the displayed position sat a median 5.2 m (arterials) / 2.9 m (residential) off the nearest road centreline on Andrew's two real logged drives — past 3.5 m for 59% / 41% of moving fixes, with sustained 6-9 m runs on Highway 27 (the map-matching study, work/mapmatch-study). The app now runs the study's design (b): an explicit driving state wrapping a gated continuity-hold matcher, DISPLAY-ONLY throughout — the snapped point is a second presentation transform inside dispShownPos, beside the 1.194 stillness hold (the hold owns rest, the snap owns driving speeds; they can never engage at once), and coverage, the trail, distance banking and the fix logger keep reading true positions exactly as before, so what gets recorded is unchanged. The constants are the study's: engage at 4 m/s or faster with a candidate road within 12 m; a rival takes the hold only by beating the held road's distance by 3 m or more on 3 consecutive fixes (the parallel-road / merge defence); the hold drops when a fix lands more than 18 m from it; one fix below 4 m/s releases outright. Two measured deviations from the study's sketch, evidence in the build record: entry is on the FIRST qualifying fix rather than after a sustained window (a 2-fix entry delay costs the arterial drive to 0.98113 and a 3-fix delay to 0.98077 against the study's 0.98148 simulated rate — both below the acceptance bar; the "sustained" condition is carried instead by re-earning the speed gate on every single fix), and design (a)'s accuracy gate (engage only on fixes reporting 20 m accuracy or better) plus an evidence timeout (more than 10 m from the held road on 5 consecutive fixes releases) ride along — both measured inert on the acceptance drives, kept as defences for wilder data. NO POPPING (Andrew's condition, confirmed on the sample): engaging and releasing ease the shown point between the true position and the centreline over about 0.8 s on the frame clock — never a teleport, never a vanish. ROAD DATA: the matcher indexes what the app itself holds — the active route's street lines (always resident) plus the shipped city pack for the city containing the fix, loaded through the app's own pack loader; packs are network-only by the service worker's design, so a failed load simply means no snap, silently, and outside the twelve pack cities and the route's streets the app holds no road geometry and nothing snaps. Stale geometry degrades the same way: no candidate nearby is always answered with the true position. HARD EXIT: Start Clean kills the hold and the blend at the tap itself, before the clean's first fix, and a clean running makes snapping impossible for its whole length. Acceptance is drive200 (new): both real drives replayed through the shipped code against the study's own road geometry — engaged fixes within 3.5 m of the true road at or above the study's simulated rates (0.98148 / 0.99069), longest wrong-road lock at most 3 fixes, zero engagement below the speed gate, the real slow turn-in released at its crossing fix, zero engagement during a clean with recording running normally, and the recorded-data invariant proven byte-identical (logger output rows, trail, banked distance and coverage, snapped vs the same replay with snapping disabled). RENUMBERED AT LANDING: this round was built and proven as Beta 1.197 on the Beta 1.196 base; while it waited, main advanced through the camera glide (1.198), the traverse fix (1.201), the pill release (1.202), the tilt + speed pill (1.203) and the retro maintenance merge, so the change was re-applied by hand onto the new main — its parts/app3.js diff verified line-for-line identical to the proven build's, and its composition against the shipped glide camera re-checked (the snap feeds dispShownPos, which the follow camera centres; the blend advances on the same frame clock the camera springs read) — and it lands under the next free number, 1.204 (the app's update check refuses a served version lower than the installed one). ROLLBACK: reverting this release's merge commit restores main dc70a295 (Beta 1.203 + the retro maintenance merge) exactly. plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.203 (PR #50, the Codex retro fix list). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.204 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.204.

## Beta 1.203 — 2026-11-15

TWO OF ANDREW'S CALLS IN ONE BUILD (he ordered them combined): THE DEFAULT VIEW TILTS, AND THE SPEED MOVES TO A PILL. THE TILT (45 degrees, at the zoom he already has): the map's default view — what the route load-in flight lands on, what the Home button returns to, and what a route switch frames — used to land flat, top-down. It now lands tilted at pitch 45, north up, at THE SAME centre and zoom the flat fit computed: Andrew's words were "keep the zoom level the way it is now… I want to keep the zoom level the same, just tilt it to 45", so the fit is still computed flat (cameraForBounds with no pitch or bearing options IS the flat fit — its centre/zoom are the replaced fitBounds' own, bit for bit) and the tilt is applied at that centre/zoom, never folded into the fit; a pitch-aware fit would have recomputed a different zoom to keep the bounds framed under perspective, which is exactly what he declined. The load-in keeps its choreography (wide point-of-interest dwell, then the slow 3000 ms flight) and the flight now tilts as it comes in; Home and a switch ease to the same landed view through the one new goHomeView; the reduced-motion / no-cameraForBounds fallback lands on the same tilted view instantly. Nothing else about the camera changes: follow, heading-up, the first-fix jump and street-level zooms are untouched, and no pitch is written anywhere in the follow path (it never wrote one — it preserves the camera's pitch, so a follow now runs at the tilted default until the user changes it by gesture). Framing was pre-proven by the tilt sample on Route 5W's real geometry at the flat-fit zoom: at pitch 45 all 770 road coordinates project inside the clear area above the bottom chrome; drive199 re-proves it in the assembled app (pitch, zoom equality against an independently computed flat fit, bearing, and every road coordinate projected inside the clear area — both themes, both phone sizes; the route box's two southern corners, which are roadless, overshoot the screen edges by 1.5px at 430x932 under the near-edge perspective magnification, while every road coordinate sits inside with 40px+ of margin) and proves the no-route boot still opens flat. THE SPEED PILL: the speed readout leaves the bottom-left corner — a 13px dark chip in its own one-off styling that showed only while moving — and becomes a pill at the top centre of the map, built from the #count pill's own design tokens (same glass, blur, border, 999px radius, padding, 12.5px/700 type and shadow, day and night) so it reads as one family with the app's other pills, per Andrew's "everything is in sync, all the text is in sync". It carries the speed ONLY (he declined kilometres driven) and its gate is the location button, full stop: startGps shows it reading "0 km/h", each fix updates the number on the same per-fix cadence the chip used, at rest or with no speed reported it reads 0 km/h instead of vanishing, and stopGps hides it — the states where it can appear are exactly the states where the chip could (the Locate watch, a clean's own GPS). The old #spd element, its CSS and its writes are removed whole — replaced, never duplicated; drive196's F6 check (the retired chip's gap over the tab bar) retires with it, its property now proven for the pill by drive199, and drive194's version pins roll to Beta 1.203. RENUMBERED AT LANDING: this round was built and proven as Beta 1.200 and queued behind the camera-glide fix; the glide fix shipped as Beta 1.201 and the pill release as Beta 1.202 before this round's turn came, and the in-app update check (parts/app4.js) refuses a served version lower than the running one — so the release lands as Beta 1.203, the next number in landing order; the change itself is the proven build, untouched. (The acceptance drive was written as drive198; the pill round's drive took that number when it landed in 1.202, so this round's drive lands as drive199.) ROLLBACK: reverting this release's merge commit restores main 6c7b31f8 (the Beta 1.202 build) exactly. No maintenance PRs since Beta 1.202. ## Beta 1.202 — 2026-11-14

THE STREETS-DONE PILL CLOCKS IN WITH THE CLEAN (Andrew: the pill under the Start button — "N of 34 · P%" with its green meter — should only appear when he presses Start Clean): through 1.198 the pill stood under the circle whenever a route was loaded, clean or no clean. It now shows ONLY while a clean is running. The rule lives in ONE place: updateCount (parts/app2.js) — which already toggles the Start/Finish/Cancel chrome from runStart in the same breath — sets the pill's display from the same predicate, and the markup's resting state is display:none (the resetBtn/cancelStartBtn inline pattern), so the pill can never paint before the first updateCount. Every lifecycle path already flows through that function: Start Clean calls it with the run live (the pill appears), Finish and Cancel call it with the run cleared (the pill leaves), a boot that restores a live run reaches it through refreshAll (the pill is up), and marking reaches it through finishChange — the count text and the meter are written whether or not the pill is shown, so marking streets outside a clean (from the panel or the pop-up) still counts internally exactly as before; the panel, the Streets header and Pace read the same state directly and are untouched. GEOMETRY, measured in the rendered page before and after (390×844, the drive harness): the pill's footprint is 37px (28px pill + 9px top margin) and the dock is bottom-anchored, so hiding the pill compacts the IDLE dock by exactly that 37px — the Start circle and the Streets/Pace buttons sit 37px lower when no clean runs, ride back up by the same 37px at Start, and settle back down at Finish/Cancel; the dock's bottom edge never moves, and the circle's bottom (idle) and the pill's bottom (running) each keep the same 16px clearance over the tab bar. The RUNNING layout — the one Andrew works in for hours — is the exact geometry 1.198 shipped (dock 141px, circle at the same y). PROOF (new drive198): the pill's presence measured in every state, never proxied — no-route boot (absent by its own resting state), route loaded / no clean (absent — the new behaviour), marking outside a clean (text and meter update, pill stays hidden), running (present, text and meter live), after Finish (absent), after Cancel (absent), night theme both ways, and a reload mid-clean (present on boot) — plus the geometry properties above asserted from live rects. drive125's T5 meter check is RE-DERIVED, not weakened: the meter's rendered fill fraction exists only while the pill is on screen, so it is now measured inside a run (Start tapped, the measurement taken on the shown pill, the start cancelled — restoring the pre-Start board); the property it proves is unchanged, and S5's count-text climb outside a run stands untouched. The first CI run of this PR (the plan escalated to the full battery because checks.yml changed) caught the same retired premise in three more checks — drive149 L2/S7 and drive150 A11 scanned document.body.innerText for the count text, and innerText only sees RENDERED text; they now read the count state from #countTxt's textContent, which updateCount writes whether or not the pill is on screen, each with its basis in the drive. RENUMBERED AT LANDING: this round was built and proven as Beta 1.199 and queued behind the camera-glide fix, which shipped first as Beta 1.201 — and the in-app update check (parts/app4.js) refuses a served version lower than the running one, so a 1.199 label would have told every 1.201 device "the server is serving an older version" and never installed this change (Codex P2 on PR #46, verified against the handler). The release therefore lands as Beta 1.202, the next number in landing order; the change itself is the proven 1.199 build, untouched. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.201 (main at 0c9bde36) exactly. No maintenance PRs since Beta 1.201. ## Beta 1.201 — 2026-11-14

THE CAMERA CATCHES UP AFTER LONG JUMPS (a defect of Beta 1.198's glide, found by the verification round the same night and fixed under the standing self-correction rule): 1.198's centre spring capped each frame's travel at (20 + the vehicle's speed) x dt, with dt clamped to 100 ms — so closing a LARGE centre gap was bounded by frame COUNT, not by time. A 643 m gap needed at least 271 frames at any cadence (measured identically at 100 ms and 3000 ms frame steps in the acceptance simulator, and reproduced in-page by the verification round against the shipped build), about 27 s at a perfect 60 fps and minutes when frames starve — where the pre-1.198 chase closed a fixed fraction of the gap per frame (5 frames at any cadence). On Andrew's phone the exposure was small — real re-anchor corrections are at most 17.6 m and close in under a second, and explicit re-centres were already exempt — but the property was real and new in 1.198: drive126's follow check failed on it twice, and drive148's resume readiness gates failed with it. THE FIX IS ONE RULE: the cap governs CORRECTIONS ONLY. followCam now engages the traverse exemption on the centre error itself — whenever the error exceeds 25 m, however the gap arose (a teleporting fix feed, a reacquisition after a long outage, a re-centre), the spring traverses uncapped until the error closes under 25 m, where the studied relative cap resumes. The 25 m threshold is the one 1.198's re-centre exemption already used — it sits above every re-anchor the follow itself generates (p99 9.9 m, max 17.6 m on the judged drive) — and the uncapped spring is the same critically-damped S-curve accepted for re-centres in 1.198: it never overshoots. MEASURED (the 1.198 acceptance simulator, with a traverse scenario added): the 643 m gap now closes to under 20 m in 9 frames at starved cadence (the 100 ms and 3000 ms arms identical — cadence-proof again) and in 60 frames / 1.0 s at 60 fps, against 271 frames / 27 s before. NON-REGRESSION, MEASURED: the judged session-2 replay through the fixed code is BYTE-IDENTICAL to 1.198's, frame for frame — the replay's centre error never exceeds 9.02 m, so the traverse rule cannot engage there — worst bearing step 4.50 deg, smoothed centre jerk p95 191 m/s^3, corner bearing error at parity (11.11 deg mean), centre time-lag 323 ms median, exactly as shipped; the re-centre returns from 100 / 300 / 1000 m and the steady 30 m/s highway tracking (bounded 9.37 m lag, the same at 30 s and 60 s) are likewise unchanged. Locally, drive126's follow checks and drive148's readiness gates — the two failures this defect caused — pass on the fixed build; the drives' remaining local noise (a night-style read in drive126, a bearing read inside a fixed 3.6 s window in drive148) is this test rig's frame starvation, classified with evidence in the round record, and the nightly full battery on CI hardware remains the standing backstop for those drives. The estimator, heading filter 2a, the stillness hold and the display position track are untouched. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.198 (main at 7a6cc3c5) exactly. plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.198. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.201 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.201.

## Beta 1.198 — 2026-11-14

THE CAMERA GLIDES (Andrew, on the shipped follow: "it does a better job, it doesn't feel like it's skipping — but it's still a little abrupt"; he asked for the transitions feathered — a glide): both camera controllers — the centre follow and the heading-up bearing — were exponential chases (close 35% of the remaining gap per 50 ms), a shape whose largest step lands on the FIRST frame of every chase: maximum velocity at the instant an error appears, then a settle. The camera-glide study replayed Andrew's own recorded session-2 drive (659 fixes) through the shipped code and measured it: worst bearing step 10.18 deg in a single frame (611 deg/s), smoothed centre jerk p95 1,021 m/s^3, every worst event within ~50 ms of a fix landing — a 1 Hz phenomenon riding on corners, not stutter. Both chases are now critically-damped springs (the SmoothDamp form — Game Programming Gems 4 ch. 1.10): the spring carries velocity between frames, so a stepped target is absorbed into an S-curve that starts at the velocity it already has, peaks mid-travel and lands soft, with frame-rate independence kept (the exp term is a polynomial in omega*dt, as the old time-based ease was). Bearing: smoothTime 0.22 s on the wrapped angle, target theta + omega*h from heading filter 2a UNCHANGED, no slew cap (one was simulated and rejected — corner error vs target blew out to a 20 deg mean; the map pointed the wrong way for seconds). Centre: smoothTime 0.32 s on the camera centre in world metres (zoom-independent, read fresh each frame so a camera moved by anything else is absorbed, never fought), target dispShownPos() UNCHANGED, with a correction cap of 20 m/s RELATIVE to the vehicle's own speed (the last accepted fix's speed — Doppler-derived, so a position step can never inflate it) so the re-anchor lunge class (the shipped chase moved the camera at 44 m/s absorbing fix 200's 17.6 m correction) can never close a correction faster than 20 m/s again — the frame's travel is clamped to (20 + the vehicle's speed) and the spring's velocity state is left as computed, so the glide resumes coherently once the cap releases. The cap's first form was absolute, and Codex's review of this very PR caught why that was wrong: above 72 km/h the camera could not even track the vehicle and fell behind without bound (~600 m in a minute at a steady 30 m/s in its mock run — the judged drive never exceeds ~16 m/s, so the study could not see it); in the relative form a steady 30 m/s target is tracked at a bounded 9.4 m lag, the same at 30 s and at 60 s, and at rest the vehicle's speed is ~0 so the cap is exactly the studied 20 m/s. The cap governs GPS-driven following ONLY: an explicit re-centre (Re-centre or Locate after panning away) is exempt until the return closes under 25 m — Codex's review caught that too (an unconditional cap strands far re-centres: 1 km at 20 m/s is a ~50 s crawl, with the button already gone); measured in simulation against a stationary target, the exempt spring return reaches under 1 m in 1.6 s from 100 m, 1.8 s from 300 m and 2.0 s from 1 km, with zero overshoot at every distance — the critically-damped shape lands soft however far it travels — and the cap re-engages for the final approach (25 m sits above every re-anchor the follow itself generates: p99 9.9 m, max 17.6 m on the judged drive). The 0.5 px / 0.3 deg deadbands become rest thresholds — a map write is skipped only when the error AND the spring's velocity are both ~0 — which deletes the deadband's ~30 Hz pan/skip limit cycle (a fine judder that fattened the jerk distribution even on straights); the render loop's parking test gains "springs at rest" beside dispSettled(), and the springs' velocities zero whenever the follow is not the camera's owner (suspended, GPS off, a teleport, northUp), so every re-engage starts from rest — on resume from a drag, the spring IS the ease back. Measured on the same replay through the shipped implementation (the study's simulator re-pointed at the shipped code as the acceptance harness): worst bearing step 10.18 → 4.50 deg, smoothed centre jerk p95 1,021 → 191 m/s^3, camera centre speed never more than 20 m/s above the vehicle's own speed (worst excess on the replay exactly 20.00 m/s; absolute peak 26 m/s, during a correction while moving ~6 m/s), corner bearing error vs theta at parity (11.1 vs 11.0 deg mean — Beta 1.194's corner win is not spent). The honest cost, stated: centre time-lag rises 111 → ~323 ms median (2.58 m at 8 m/s, under a metre at driveway pace; offset p95 ~5 px at z17 against the app's own ~47 px follow tolerance), and settle-vs-target in course-step bursts lengthens (~2.3 s → ~3.4 s) while error vs theta — where the vehicle is actually pointing — is unchanged. NOT in this round, deliberately: the bearing target still steps at 1 Hz (nothing presentation-side can remove that without touching the estimator; the spring absorbs each step instead of snapping at it), and the puck's own display track still lunges on the biggest re-anchors (the dispOff ~0.19 s decay, upstream of the camera — the cap keeps the CAMERA at ≤ 20 m/s through those events; the decay is a separate follow-up needing its own judging pass, because the stillness hold reads the same track). The estimator, heading filter 2a, the stillness hold, the display position track, northUp's behaviour and followPaused are untouched. Rollback: revert this merge → Beta 1.196 (main at db17e6c5) exactly. plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.196. ## Beta 1.195 — 2026-11-14

THE GPS DEBUG LOG BECOMES A SWITCH (Andrew's call, reversing this morning's removal the same day it shipped): the removal (PR #41) merged and was live for ten minutes before Andrew redirected — the log is his debugging tool and it STAYS, so this build's first commit reverts PR #41 whole and this entry's change converts the restored logger. It now records only behind a switch: "GPS debug log" in Settings → Backup, rendered by the sheet's own switch pattern and re-rendered from the runtime flag every time the Backup pane opens. The flag is runtime-only state — a plain in-memory switch initialized OFF at every boot, never written into the settings record, never carried in a backup, and impossible for a restore to enable, because no stored representation of it exists anywhere in the format; asking the app to log is a deliberate act Andrew repeats each app run. Recording is gated at the one entry point (fixLogRaw): off means a fix is never recorded and the log's storage is never touched, on (even flipped mid-watch) records from the next fix, and flipping off stops recording without deleting anything — the recorded sessions stay whole for the backup export he makes after the drive. The caps fall to the debugging scale: FIXLOG_MAX 36,000 → 3,600 fixes (one hour at 1 Hz) and FIXLOG_MAX_CHARS 1,850,000 → 200,000 characters, sized from the codec's measured worst case (~46.0 characters per fix in the 1.190 soak → ~165,600 at the new fix cap, the rest margin; arithmetic in parts/app1.js). The e3 codec, locate-on session segmentation, the whole-replace backup ride and the no-display-surface boundary are unchanged. Proof: drive194 re-derived (caps at the new numbers) plus new toggle sections — cold-open off with a driven watch leaving storage byte-identical, on-recording with session segmentation, mid-session off with the data intact and exportable, and a backup cycle that never enables logging on the receiving boot. plus 1 PR since Beta 1.194 (PR #41, the removal reverted by this build's first commit).
