## Beta 1.194 — 2026-11-14
THE MAP STOPS CHASING CORNERS, AND THE PUCK STOPS TWITCHING (both from judging Andrew's own recorded drives, round 3): HEADING FILTER 2a REPLACES THE HEADING SMOOTHER. Through 1.193 the heading-up bearing came from the 1.187 smoother (a time-normalized exponential average of the reported course); on Andrew's real drives the phone's course moves in STEPS at corners, and the smoother chased each step for 5–9 seconds — in one 11-minute drive among houses, 52 of 659 fixes sat more than 20 degrees off the direction of travel, peaking at 74 degrees. The new filter carries a heading AND a turn rate (state [θ, ω]): it predicts θ += ω·dt with process noise q = 400, measures the course with speed-dependent noise (σ = 6 + 18/max(v, 0.5) degrees), damps freak innovations through a soft gate (an innovation beyond 3√S is heard at 9× the noise) WITH a step escape (a second consecutive same-sign large innovation is accepted at face value — two in a row are a manoeuvre, not noise), clamps the turn rate to ±40°/s, holds θ still under 1 m/s while the rate decays, and the camera reads θ + ω·h (h capped at 1 s). Replayed through the shipped code on both recorded drives (drive197): session-2 fixes over 20 degrees of error fall 52 → 5, corner settle drops from 5–15 fixes to 0–1 on 13 of 14 episodes, the post-reverse course snap settles in 1 fix instead of ~9 seconds of chase, and straight-line error falls from 3.13 to 0.20 degrees RMS. NO REVERSAL GUARD WAS BUILT, deliberately: Andrew labelled the one recorded reverse himself (he was backing up), and through the whole reverse the reported course stayed frozen at the pre-reverse heading — which is the nose heading a heading-up camera wants, so the shipped behaviour does nothing harmful; the proposed flip-detector never fires on that mechanism and its only possible trigger is the post-reverse exit step, where holding would pin the camera ~176 degrees wrong. The guard is closed, not deferred. THE DISPLAY STILLNESS HOLD: the puck twitched slightly while Andrew stood still — not GPS wander (the phone pins its position at rest; measured raw scatter 0.08–0.28 m) but the DISPLAY position re-settling in sub-metre steps the raw fixes never made. The fix is presentation-only: once the reported speed has been under 0.5 m/s for 2 fixes AND the last 3 display steps were each under 0.3 m (the settled test — it stays out of the arrival glide), the rendered point freezes; it releases on 1 fix at 0.8 m/s, 2 fixes at 0.5 m/s, or the raw position moving more than 1.5 m from the held point on 2 consecutive fixes. Proven on the same replay: the hold engages in all 4 recorded stops and nowhere else in either drive (in particular not in the pause before the reverse), the rendered point is bit-frozen while engaged, exit jumps measure 0.16–1.18 m, and the one real 2.86 m creep between stops is released at its second fix with a 0.31 m jump. Only what is DRAWN sees the held point (the puck, its beam and ring, the camera-follow target); street coverage, the trail, the distance counter and the fix logger read true positions exactly as before. NO ZUPT (estimator-level stationary averaging) was built either — its target problem measured 20–70× smaller on this phone than the simulation that proposed it. The process-noise configuration q = 400 ships as judged: the round-2 simulator's heading-burst guard passes at 400 (3 fixes over 15 degrees of camera error, the same count as the old smoother; its peak is higher — the turn-rate state leans into a repeated innovation — and the mandated q = 100 fallback was measured to give the same count, so it was not taken). STRUCTURE: two commits inside one PR — heading filter first, display hold second — so either reverts independently. The position estimator, coverage, banking, the trail window and the puck's rendering are untouched; the fix logger is untouched too — everything it was kept alive to judge has now been judged, and its removal is a separate round. ROLLBACK: reverting this release's merge commit restores main afc61cd4 (Beta 1.193 plus the docs PR) exactly. plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.193. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.194 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.194.


## Beta 1.193 — 2026-11-14
THE TRAIL BECOMES WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS (Andrew's adopted design): through 1.192 the app kept every GPS point of every clean (up to an 8,000-point cap), drew it as a blue line on the map, and baked a thinned copy of it into each saved clean. Andrew's call: his trail is the streets he marks done — the line goes. THE TRAIL IS NOW A BACKEND-ONLY ROLLING WINDOW: it keeps only the live run's points from its last 60 minutes (TRAIL_WINDOW_S = 3600, parts/app1.js), windowed by the one rule in trailWindow at append (acceptFix), at load, at Start (a fence that evicts anything not of this run), at restore, and cleared at Finish; its only remaining job is to stand behind the run-distance counter as its fallback. The drawn surface is removed whole — the 'trail' source, the 'trail-line' layer and drawTrail are gone, and the map shows no path — and saved cleans carry no archived path any more (buildSnap's bake and its thinning helpers are retired); cleans saved before this update keep the paths they already have, and the Pace detail still speaks of them. THE COUNTER IS HARDENED TO MATCH: with only an hour of trail behind it, the banked counter must never be the stale part — persistRunDist now banks on EVERY recorded fix (the 30 s / 25 m throttle is gone; the record is a few dozen characters overwritten in place, so the storage footprint is unchanged), and a second copy of the record rides its own key (LS.rundist2), written only by the trail-flush path in flushAux (app1) — a different writer, trigger and cadence from the per-fix write, so the two copies fail independently. RECONCILIATION, RESTATED HONESTLY: adoption reads the primary, then the mirror, then the trail derivation, and takes the maximum — every candidate is a lower bound of the run's true total (a bank only ever accumulates real recorded segments; the window's derivation sums a subset of the run's recorded pairs under the same pair rule), so the max can raise a stale bank toward truth inside the window and can never overstate it or speak for a span the window does not cover. The 1.167 capped flag retires with the capped trail it described; legacy records carrying it are read for their run and metres, the field ignored. THE NAMED RESIDUAL: if both counter copies are lost, recovery is exactly the window's own distance — the last hour, nothing more is promised. THE KILL TEST (drive195) runs a simulated clean and kills/reloads the app at simulated hours 1, 5 and 7+: the recovered distance matches ground truth within a few metres; with the primary record deleted the mirror recovers within the same tolerance; with both deleted, recovery equals the window's own distance exactly; the drive also proves no trail surface exists, that a new saved clean carries no path, and that the window holds at every kill. BACKUP FORMAT, STATED PLAINLY: the backup's shape does not change — the trail key still carries an array of [lat, lng, ts] points — but its SCOPE does: a backup now carries at most the live run's last hour of trail points instead of every clean's, and a restore windows whatever trail a backup (this version's or any earlier one) brings to the pack's own live run before writing it — a pre-1.193 backup restores cleanly, its archived cleans keep their paths, and its trail lands as the window rule allows (a pack whose backup carries no live run lands an empty trail). Restores of every prior format remain covered by the standing restore drives. The fix logger is untouched by design — it stays at full size until the post-judging removal round; street coverage, the board, pace math and the estimator are untouched. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 514b826a07 (the Beta 1.192 build) exactly. No maintenance PRs since Beta 1.192. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.193 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.193.


## Beta 1.192 — 2026-11-14
THE BOTTOM BAR TAKES THE STANDARD HEIGHT (Andrew, from his side-by-side screenshot of Snow Route next to another app — "my bottom menu bar is larger and sits up higher... lower mine to the same height"): measured first, in the shipped code and in his screenshot. The bar was never floating — it is pinned to the screen's bottom edge exactly like the other app's — it was TALLER: 56px of content zone over the home-indicator safe area (~90pt total on his phone) against the standard iOS tab bar's 49px (~83pt). The whole 7px difference was padding, none of it content: the 24px icons and 10px labels were already standard size. THE CHANGE (parts/head.html, CSS only): #tabBar padding 6px/calc(6px + safe area) -> 4px/calc(3px + safe area), .tabItem padding 4px 0 2px -> 3px 0 1px — content zone 56px -> 49px. THE OFFSET AUDIT (every screen-bottom-anchored constant in the stylesheet judged on whether it derives from the bar's height; this repo's history holds no prior bar-height change to copy a precedent from, so each verdict rests on the element's geometry and the drives that measure it): the three floaters that hug the bar's top edge with a fixed clearance gap ride down the same 7px, rendered gaps preserved exactly (measured in the assembled page, before and after: the rendered bar stands 2px over its design zone — the label's line box — so the rendered gaps read 2px under the constant differences) — #dock 74px -> 67px (rendered gap 16px; the cancel button rides the dock by construction and moves with it), #spd 76px -> 69px (rendered gap 18px), #attrChip 68px -> 61px (rendered gap 10px). THE SNOW PEAK FOLLOWS (parts/brand-snow.js): the splash snow's settled bank was deliberately peaked at the bar's rendered height in Beta 1.161 — crest exactly as tall as the bar — so its hard-coded peak and its fade anchor follow the bar down, 58 -> 51, and drive180's SNOW9 pin is re-pinned to the 51 the drive itself measures on the built page; leave the peak at 58 and the bank would have crested 7px above the shorter bar. Three constants do NOT ride, deliberately: .banner (236px) and #mapNote (240px) clear the whole bottom chrome stack (bar + dock + the cancel button above the dock's circle), not the bar alone — after the dock's ride the stack's top drops ~7px and the banners simply gain clearance, and drive115's no-overlap-with-the-bar check gains margin instead of losing it; #toast (120px) is a screen-level placement (z-index 10001, it must read over even the full-screen menus where no bar exists — Beta 1.93 F03) and is not a bar-clearance gap. PROOF, measured in the rendered page per the design-QA rule (drive196, new): the bar's content zone measures exactly 49px in day AND night; all five tab items are tapped at their corners and centres and fire their actions; the dock, speed chip and attribution chip sit at their exact pre-change rendered gaps (16/18/10px) above the bar's NEW top edge; the banner zone and toast positions are byte-identical to before; nothing overlaps the bar. The tappable geometry, measured rather than assumed: the tab items stretch to fill the bar's content zone, so their height follows the zone — 46px before, 44px after — a 2px cost, not the 7px the padding arithmetic alone suggested; the 7px came out of the bar's padding, and the items keep a full-height touch target. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 31a0c85c (the Beta 1.191 build) exactly. No maintenance PRs since Beta 1.191. ## Beta 1.191 — 2026-11-14
THE SPLASH LOSES THE TILE (Andrew's pick from the splash-icon sample, 2026-11-14: "only the main truck and snow part of the icon... so it looks part of the blue screen instead of on top"). The opening screen no longer shows the app icon as a blue square floating on the splash blue: #splashIcon now paints the icon's ARTWORK ALONE — the truck, the blade and the snow, on a transparent background, with no rounded corners and no drop shadow — sitting directly on the splash gradient. It is subtraction, not redesign: the artwork is Andrew's own asset-pack foreground (Android/adaptive-foreground-1024.png, the production rendering of his master vectors with the background left out), cropped tight to the artwork's alpha bounds (605x500 of the 1024 canvas) and downscaled to 512x423 as the new repo asset splash-artwork-512.png; scripts/assemble.py inlines THAT file at the splash placeholder (the token is renamed to match), and icon-512.png — still the PWA and home-screen icon, which must keep its square — is untouched, as are the apple-touch-icon and favicon. The inline payload SHRINKS: 53,122 bytes / 70,832 base64 chars against the icon's 65,234 / 86,980. GEOMETRY: the artwork displays at the tile's old 168px width; its height is its own (168 x 423/512 = 138.8px, where the tile forced 168), and because #brandLayer centres the brand block in the viewport, the artwork's centre lands where the tile's centre landed — measured on the rendered box in drive180: centre (210, 449.99) against the viewport centre (210, 450) at 420x900, offsets 0.00px / 0.01px. THE CHOREOGRAPHY IS UNTOUCHED: the icon's own 1.25s fade (splashIconIn), the pre-1.2s dark window, the 2700ms boot floor, the cut at the flip, the snow canvas and the plain-blue launch stills all behave byte-identically, and the reduced-motion path still shows the artwork static from the first frame (verified in the round's own emulation proof: animation none, opacity 1 at 600ms, same box, same centre). PROOF (drive180): ICONSIZE is RE-PINNED from the tile's 168x168 box to the artwork box (168 wide, 138.8 tall, +/-2 — the basis stated in the check is the shipped asset's measured aspect); CENTRE/HOLD1/SEQ1/SEQ2/S8 needed no re-pin and pass on the new box; two NEW checks judge the tile's absence on the rendered page — NOTILE1 (computed border-radius 0px, box-shadow none, the inlined image's natural size 512x423, and the asset's own corner/edge alphas at their measured transparent values) and NOTILE2 (a hold screenshot: the pixel at a measured-transparent point of the box matches the splash gradient beside the box within 3 per channel, bound 14 — the tile build paints its opaque navy there). On the unmodified tile build all three changed checks FAIL, as they must. ## Beta 1.190 — 2026-11-13
THE FIX LOG LEARNS HOW LONG A CLEAN IS (Andrew's correction, the night 1.189 shipped): the raw fix log's cap was sized off a 2.7-hour observed clean — 12,000 fixes, about 3.3 hours at 1 Hz — but Andrew's cleans run 6 to 8 HOURS, and a lone session longer than the cap is head-trimmed, so the first hours of a long clean would have silently fallen out of the very log that exists to judge the GPS honestly. FIXLOG_MAX is now 36,000 fixes (the one named constant, parts/app1.js) — 10 hours at 1 Hz, so one full 8-hour clean (28,800 fixes) fits whole inside a single session with a quarter of the cap to spare, and the eviction policy is unchanged (oldest sessions whole; only a lone session beyond the new cap head-trims). THE STORAGE PROBLEM, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED: at 1.189's plain-JSON stored form the log measures ~169 characters per fix at full precision, so 36,000 fixes would have measured ~6.1 million characters — and the budget that binds is WebKit's: localStorage allows 5 MB per origin and persists strings as UTF-16, two bytes per character, so the WHOLE origin holds 2,500,000 characters (the quota constant in WebKit's LocalStorageManager; webkit.org bug 245479). The stored form is therefore now a lossless packed encoding ("e3", parts/app3.js): the record is an object { e: 3, s: [...] } whose sessions are byte streams of varint deltas packed seven bits per character — millisecond timestamp differences, coordinate differences taken on the doubles' IEEE-754 bit patterns (decode restores the EXACT double, bit for bit), the estimator outputs as micro-degree integer deltas (they are 6dp / 1dp by construction, so the restoration is exact), the accuracy / speed / course fields as bit-pattern deltas, and the run stamp written only when it changes. Every raw field is recoverable exactly — nulls included, refused fixes included — and the encoding lives only at the storage boundary: the logger's in-memory sessions, its hooks and its trim are untouched, and fixLogDecodeSessions is the ONE lens for the boot load and the backup validator alike. TWO CAPS, BOTH ENFORCED BY THE TRIM: FIXLOG_MAX (36,000 fixes) and FIXLOG_MAX_CHARS (1,850,000 characters of stored record, parts/app1.js) — the logger keeps an exact per-entry byte account as it appends, so the character cap is judged on the packed size itself, and a stream denser than any model is trimmed by size instead of breaking the quota mid-clean. The arithmetic: 1,850,000 + the trail at its cap (~250,000 characters) + a 250,000-character board/settings/history allowance = 2,350,000 ≤ the 2,500,000 the quota holds. Measured: ~46 characters/fix on drive194's worst-case stream (1,655,295 characters at the full cap, written through the real save path), ~48 at transit speed on a stepwise-accuracy stream, ~44 at plow pace, ~33 parked — drive194 asserts the measured characters against the quota arithmetic, proves the trim's estimate never under-reads the real length, proves a high-entropy flood is trimmed by the character cap before the fix cap, and proves a synthetic 8-hour session (28,800 fixes) survives whole with zero trimmed. (This entry's first form at the gate measured the same budget in the wrong unit — a JSON delta encoding at ~3.7 million characters would still have crossed the quota on its own; Codex's review caught it, and the packed form above is the fix.) COMPATIBILITY BOTH WAYS: the decoder accepts the v1 array form, so every 1.189 record and backup loads and restores as before (the canonical validator decodes either form, applies both caps, and re-writes e3); and because the e3 record is an object where 1.189's loader required an array, a 1.189 build meeting an e3 record ignores the log whole rather than misreading packed data as absolute fixes. One honest cost: a save now encodes before it stringifies, so the throttled flush does more work per write — it stays on the trail's cadence and forced flushes still always land it. Nothing about the estimator, the camera, the trail or runDist changes; the log still has no display, export or share surface and leaves the phone only inside Andrew's own backup JSON. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main af7cf9fdec85 (the Beta 1.189 build plus PR #30's test hardening) exactly. Plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.189 (PR #30, drive156 B5/B6 test hardening). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.190 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.190.

## Beta 1.189 — 2026-11-13
THE RAW FIX LOG (Andrew's pick on the third-seat pass, 2026-11-13: "build the raw-fix logger first, then judge everything on real drives"): every GPS verdict this app carries — the drift floor, the turn lag, the stop wander — rests on a simulator whose fixes are independent white noise, while a real phone's fixes come out of the OS's own filter and are autocorrelated over tens of seconds; the floors may be the model's, not the phone's. This release starts the honest measurement: a debugging instrument in the trail's family records EVERY fix the watch delivers, captured in acceptFix BEFORE any gate can refuse it (the finite/on-Earth check, the out-of-order drop, the accuracy gate), the raw input exactly as delivered — timestamp, latitude, longitude, reported accuracy, speed, course, nulls kept (a stationary fix reporting no speed is data) — with the estimator's outputs for the same fix beside it (filtered position, display position, smoothed heading), so a future replay compares raw against filtered without re-deriving either; a gated fix is recorded too, its output slot left null, because the refusal is itself replay data. The stream is segmented by SESSION (one locate-on period — startGps opens one, stopGps closes it, the stale-watch restart honestly opens a fresh one), each session stamps the app version that recorded it, and every fix carries the Start-Clean run stamp live at its delivery (0 = no clean running), so cleans are marked inside the stream. THE BOUNDARY (Andrew's standing debugging-data rule, the trail's identically): the log lives on-device only and rides inside his own backup JSON — the normal backup reads it raw under the same B04 strictness as the settings record (a failed read or an unlanded flush refuses the export), the recovery backup carries the live log from memory — and there is NO display, export or share surface for it anywhere in the app; it leaves the phone only inside a backup he exports and hands over himself. It is debug data, not clean data: capped at FIXLOG_MAX = 12,000 fixes (one named constant, parts/app1.js — about 3.3 hours of 1 Hz fixes, a full clean plus margin) with oldest-session eviction (a lone oversize session is head-trimmed), where cleans stay unlimited. Persistence follows the trail's discipline exactly: an in-memory append per fix (the only per-fix cost), a fixlog dirty flag in app1's one doorway, and the throttled flushAux write on the trail's cadence, forced flushes always landing it. Every logging entry point is failure-isolated — a logging fault fails silent and can never break the GPS path — and the estimator, camera, trail and runDist are untouched: logging observes acceptFix, it does not participate in it. BACKUP COMPATIBILITY: the field is additive like settings before it — a 1.188 backup (no fixlog field) restores cleanly and leaves the device's log untouched under the missing-key rule; a 1.189 backup restored into an older build is ignored as an unknown field; in this build the field is validated by canonicalFixLog (the settings validator's sibling: the loader's own rules, the writers' cap applied, canonical re-serialization), written inside the same verified write set with its key in the rollback scope, REPLACING the device log whole — never merged, so no C-01-style split of a resident record by an incoming boundary is possible — and restoreFenceDown re-arms its dirty flag with the trail's, so fixes logged during a failed restore's window are carried by the next flush. PROOF (new drive194): synthetic fixes fed through the REAL watch path (a stubbed geolocation whose captured callback is startGps's own) are recorded verbatim — every raw field, nulls included, in order, gated fixes included with null outputs; the recorded estimator outputs equal the app's own gpsPos/dispPos/smHeading after the same fixes; the session stamps the live version and fixes carry the live run stamp; the log persists across a reload; a backup round-trip carries it field-for-field and an old-format backup restores clean with the resident log untouched; the cap holds under a 12,050-fix flood (oldest session evicted whole, a lone session head-trimmed to its newest 12,000); and a stationary-noise sequence still holds the dot inside drive127's corridor, with the full GPS family (127/133 and the restore drives) re-run on the same tree. ROLLBACK: reverting this release's merge commit restores main c780d2e64fa2 (the Beta 1.188 build) exactly. Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.188. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.189 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.189.

## Beta 1.188 — 2026-11-13
THE PUCK IS DRAWN, NOT BUILT (Andrew's pick from the touchable demo page, 2026-11-13 — the shader rendering of the approved coin over the geometry coin and the shipping puck): the two Beta 1.182 fill-extrusion layers (gps-puck-white / gps-puck-blue, parts/app2.js) are replaced by ONE custom WebGL layer (gps-puck-shader) that draws the coin in SCREEN SPACE with a signed-distance fragment shader, and pushGps no longer emits puck features at all. WHY THE OLD ONE HAD TO GO: MapLibre projects geometry vertices into single-precision tile coordinates, so at z19 the extrusion's edges stair-stepped no matter the segment count; the ground-disc size had to be clamped into a 14–110 px band and still ballooned to the cap at max zoom; and as a fellow extrusion the puck lost the depth fight to any 3D building in front of it (field report 2026-11-13, proven in the GPS investigation: a real 10.5 m house 7 m away hid ~95% of it). THE COIN, AS APPROVED: diameter 5.4% of canvas width at EVERY zoom, pitch and bearing; the blue core 62% of D recessed under a rim that stands ~1 px proud (wall lift 0.14 × D, blue top 0.10 × D at pitch 60); face height cos(pitch)^1.47, so the silhouette is 0.50 × D at pitch 60 and a true circle at pitch 0; app blue #2C4AD2; edges anti-aliased analytically (~1 device px) at any zoom. Depth test OFF and depth mask off — buildings can never cover it. The centre is project(dispPos) read inside render() every frame, so the puck cannot lag the camera; every piece of GL state the draw touches is snapshotted and restored. Visibility is the retired puck's rule exactly — drawn while dispPos is set (locate-off keeps the last position marked with the beam gone; no fix yet draws nothing) — and the beam, the accuracy ring, the estimator and drive156's quarantine are untouched; Beta 1.187's app3.js changes (the time-normalized heading step, the stopped-fix trail guard) are carried over verbatim, and the geometry puck with its 14–110 px clamp machinery is retired. PROOF (new drive193, rendered PIXELS — the failure properties themselves, never proxies): the core width stays at 0.62 × D at z14/16/18/19 × pitch 0/60 and the full footprint width is frozen with it; the silhouette, measured on the visibility-flip difference's strong channel (a >90 channel swing only the opaque body can make — the ≤0.30-alpha shadow cannot), reads 1.013 × D flat and 0.507 × D at pitch 60 (z19: 0.485 × D); a synthetic 20 m building placed camera-side at z18/pitch 60 leaves the core essentially whole, where the extrusion read ~0–5%; through an easeTo flight the centroid stays on the projected point (in-frame samples worst 0.33 css px against inter-frame camera travels up to 173 px); the night restack renders it at the same size; locate-off keeps the last position marked. Two of drive193's own instruments were wrong on their first runs and were fixed at the same strength, debug records kept with the round: its rim-colour classifier could not see the rim's near-pure-white lit top (white fails a blue-lean test) and let single pale basemap pixels stretch the tilt box to 1.32 × D — replaced by the strong-difference body above — and its first flight cut travelled 224 css px at MapLibre's 512-px tile scale and flew the puck off the frame's west edge mid-flight (the instrumented render log showed the layer drawing faithfully at an off-canvas point) — the flight now travels ~75 css px and stays in frame throughout. The rendering contract's drives are re-derived at the same strength, never weakened: drive124 (the marker renders at the fix and survives marking and a basemap restack — now the shader layer), drive125 (the theme-swap snapshot's gps presence), drive129 (beam discipline; C5 pins the retired shadow's absence alongside the puck's presence), drive133 (the live marker in view-only mode). One local-only note: drive125's dot-zoom place-label checks fail under this machine's starved tile stream on the PRISTINE 1.186 tree identically (26/28, same two checks) — environmental, tree-independent; CI is their authority. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main b3c2662ec3 (Beta 1.187 + the SAFO docs graft) exactly. Plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.187. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.188 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.188.

## Beta 1.187 — 2026-11-13
THE GPS PACKAGE, EVIDENCE-GATED (Andrew approved the five-item package from the 2026-11-13 field-test investigation the same day; every item was priced in the investigation's own harness — the noise-model simulation over the real Route 5W centrelines, 10 seeds, the estimator extracted verbatim — before shipping, and two of the five passed their measurements. This release ships exactly those two; the three that failed are recorded at the end, not shipped). (TURN A) THE HEADING SMOOTHER NOW RUNS ON TIME, NOT FIX COUNT (parts/app3.js updateHeading): the course step was 0.35 per fix regardless of spacing, so bursty delivery (two fixes 0.15 s apart, then a 3 s gap — how phones actually deliver under load) stepped it twice in a blink or starved it for seconds; sized by the real interval between fixes (retention 0.65^dt, dt clamped 0.05–3 s) it is step-for-step identical at a steady 1 Hz, and in the harness the bursty peak turn error falls 62.5 → 55.6 degrees, bursty turn RMS 32.9 → 28.4, runs still unsettled 8 s after the turn 9/10 → 6/10, the mid-corner speed-dip RMS 24.0 → 21.4, with regular cadence untouched (peak 59.0 → 60.1, inside seed noise). (STOP B) A STOPPED FIX NO LONGER RECORDS (parts/app1.js GPS_STILL_MPS + the acceptFix trail guard, parts/app3.js): a fix reporting under 0.5 m/s banks no run-distance segment and adds no trail point — a parked truck's GPS wander was banking a median 138 phantom metres a minute into saved cleans; the harness prices the guard at 13.6 phantom m/min (the residue is distance honestly re-measured between the last recorded point and the next moving fix). The position path is untouched — the dot follows every accepted fix exactly as before; coverage still runs on stopped fixes (a truck edging a driveway at creep pace keeps earning its streets); a fix reporting no speed is no evidence of stillness and records as before; and the next moving fix measures its segment from the last RECORDED point, so genuine distance survives — at working pace the guard is close to invisible (0.6–1.0 m/s traces keep 96–143 of their 150 points). PRICED AND NOT SHIPPED: (i) driving the camera from the Kalman filter's own velocity (the investigation's TURN B) LOST its A/B in both forms tried, with and without course extrapolation — peak turn error 59 → 88 degrees and straight-line RMS up to 15.7 degrees at 2 m/s, because at plowing speeds the filter's velocity direction is noisier and laggier than the phone's Doppler course; an extrapolated-OS-course variant also lost on straight-line RMS and is likewise left out. (ii) The stationary position hold (STOP A) failed both of its acceptance gates: frozen at a deceleration-biased estimate it made stop wander WORSE (RMS 5.9 → 8.2 m, worst excursion 22.5 → 34.1 m), its restart re-settle merely matched the warm filter it replaced (15.19 s vs the 15.21 s baseline it had to beat), and it froze the dot during genuine 0.6 m/s work 68% of the time. (iii) Tightening the accuracy gate to 28 m (the drift item) moved every drift metric slightly the wrong way (median 1.67 → 1.76 m, p90 4.39 → 4.54 m, worst-run excursion 51 → 56 m): the filter already weights fixes by their reported accuracy, so hard rejection only discards information and lengthens coasts; GPS_ACCURACY_M stays 65. TESTS: new tests/browser/selftest-gps-package.mjs (16 fixtures — the shipped updateHeading verbatim: 1 Hz equality with the legacy step, burst/gap behaviour, the dt clamp, the speed/null gates; the shipped trail block verbatim: stopped fixes record nothing but still cover, distance re-measured from the last recorded point, null-speed and at-threshold fixes record; both constants pinned) wired into build-and-test; drive133's march now reports a moving 3 m/s (its old speed-0 feed claimed travel between points at a reported standstill — a physical impossibility the guard correctly treats as stopped; the drive's premise is a moving clean). No backup, storage or trail format changes; unlimited cleans retention untouched; the puck is not touched (its own round owns it). ROLLBACK: reverting this release's merge commit restores main 0c66e05fe5 (the Beta 1.186 build) exactly. Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.186. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.187 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.187.

## Beta 1.186 — 2026-11-13
THE STORAGE FIX ROUND: THREE ADJUDICATED FINDINGS CLOSED (the three parked storage/restore findings from the Ponytail audit and the first Carbon sweep, adjudicated against the 1.183 code with reproducing harnesses — all three REAL, all low severity — and approved by Andrew on 2026-11-13 under the review-openness rule; the sweep A-01 entry is P1's same root cause in its restore-window form). (P2) A SAVED CLEAN'S DISTANCE CAN NO LONGER COME UP SHORT (parts/app1.js flushAux + parts/app3.js adoptRunDist): the run-distance counter persisted on a 30 s / 25 m throttle, but the forced flush that fires at pagehide, visibility-hidden and the route switch never carried it, and adoption believed a matching banked record verbatim — so a mid-run reload inside the window resumed a figure up to <25 m stale and buildSnap baked it into the archived clean, while the trail itself was always fully flushed. A forced flush now banks the counter past its throttle (guarded on a run in flight — an ungated call minted the zero record into a route's slot on every switch, caught by drive184's round-trip — beside the saveSnap retry, in the typeof-probe house pattern), and adoption reconciles a matching UNCAPPED record against the surviving trail, whose measure is complete for the run; a CAPPED record is exempt — its trail is truncated by design and the counter stays the sole authority, the max() never runs there. The archived km usually rounded the loss away; the counter is nevertheless now right at the source. (C-01) MARKERS WITHOUT THEIR TRAIL NO LONGER SPLIT THE TRAIL THAT STAYS (parts/app4.js, restoreBackup): the 1.73 C06 guard cleared a replaced trail's markers when a backup brought a trail without markers; the mirror case survived — a backup carrying session markers but NO trail (a genuine shape, not just a crafted one: the exporter carries each field only when its key exists, and markers are born at Start while the trail key is born at the first accepted fix, so a started-but-never-fixed clean exports markers alone) laid those markers over the resident trail, splitting it at a session boundary that never happened on it for drawTrail and the GPX walk (distances unaffected — trailKm walks segments without markers). The restore now drops such markers from the write set entirely — and leaves the resident markers untouched, since they describe the trail that stays (C06 remains the true mirror: there the trail itself is replaced, so its markers are cleared in the write set). (P1) THE RESTORE FENCE RISES BEFORE THE SNAPSHOT (parts/app4.js, restoreBackup): through 1.185 the `restoring` fence was set only on the success path, after the commit — leaving the write stage's one yield, phase 1's awaited IndexedDB puts for a backup carrying created routes, unfenced. A GPS flush landing there wrote live records the snapshot had already frozen; if a later entries write failed, the rollback rewrote every scoped key from that snapshot and silently discarded the live write under a "nothing was changed" message (worst case narrow — it needs a created-routes backup, an actively recording clean and a mid-restore write failure at once, and the loss usually self-healed on the next flush — but the message lied, which is the defect). The fence now rises immediately before the snapshot, so flushAux and saveAll refuse for the whole write stage and storage cannot diverge from the snapshot before the commit; the restore's own writes go through txSet/txDel and upPut directly and are unfenced by construction. Every failure exit (snapshot read, phase 1, the entries loop) ends through a single doorway that clears the fence and re-arms the trail/session/coverage dirty flags, so GPS work that arrived during the window flushes from memory afterwards — and the two rollback paths, which through 1.185 treated concurrent live work inconsistently (the phase-1 exit preserved what the entries exit discarded), now treat it identically. The Beta 1.166 phase ordering, the backup format and the post-commit fence (drive169's ground since 1.147) are untouched. Proof, in the battery's own form: drive169 gains the C-01 set (markers-only restore drops the incoming markers and preserves the resident ones, on a one-clean trail and on a two-clean trail whose markers alone keep its cleans apart; the carried-pair control restores untouched) and a P1 fault-injection section (created-route backup, upPut gated to hold the phase-1 window open deterministically, an in-window fix + flush refused under the risen fence, a refused start delete, then the rollback's truth and the in-window point surviving the post-failure flush); drive187 gains the P2 trio (stale uncapped bank reconciles to the trail measure, capped bank stays authority, forced flush banks past the throttle while the throttle itself still holds). Every new check fails on the unfixed 1.185 tree and passes here. GATE ROUND (the PR's own review, before merge): two Codex findings against the round's own fixes, both adjudicated real and fixed in this same PR. (i) The P2 forced bank could write the UN-ADOPTED zero counter over a stored record in the reload window (runStart restored at boot, adoption lazy, a pagehide before the first fix or buildSnap) — for a capped run that loss was permanent; persistRunDist now adopts before it writes (a no-op for every caller that already guarantees the match), and drive187 gains the P2d check for the window. (ii) The P1 failure doorway re-armed trail/session/coverage but not PRIMARY, so a street mark made during the restore's write stage — refused by the fence, which by the 1.147 design arms nothing — was never retried after a failed restore and a reload lost it; the doorway now re-arms primary too, and drive169 gains the P1b fault-injection section. (iii) A third finding, against the C-01 fix's first cut: it cleared the RESIDENT session markers along with the incoming ones, so a markers-only restore over a two-clean resident trail silently erased the boundaries between its cleans (their points sit inside trailSegments' join thresholds); the guard now drops the incoming markers from the write set instead of writing an empty record over the resident one, and drive169's C-01 set gains the two-clean preservation check. The foreign-write census also re-pinned in the round (the P1 fence's two new adjudicated sites are the census's only count change, verified pair-for-pair). The A1 type pilot also gained the persistRunDist cross-part declaration its saveSnap sibling already had (the pilot compiles app1 alone; the declaration file is never assembled into the page). Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.185. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.186 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.186.

## Beta 1.185 — 2026-11-13
FIVE BUGS FOUND BY AN OUTSIDE BUG HUNT, ADJUDICATED AND LANDED WITH PROOF (the fixes come from PR #23 — an external Claude session Andrew ran for fun and handed over for manual review; every claim was adjudicated against this code before landing, verdict 5 for 5 real and 5 for 5 sound, full record in the assessment kept with the goal; Andrew approved the merge with two conditions — land after Beta 1.184 with the build re-run, and proof coverage first — both met by this PR, which supersedes PR #23 per the PR #11 → #12 pattern: the fixes were re-applied at the parts level onto the 1.184 tree and the generated files rebuilt, never carried over). (1) THE CROSSING SCAN NO LONGER ABANDONS ITS POST (parts/engine.js, edgeRunSlices): when a finished side's line is allowed to run unclipped to a junction vertex because this street's own band fills the far arm, the code checks that the line properly crosses NO other street's marked line first — but the per-street loop broke out entirely at this street's own name, so every street listed after it went unchecked, and if its own name came first NO street was checked and the line painted across a marked line it should have respected. The self-name case now continues the scan; only a found crossing breaks it — the same shape as the sibling veto loop in the same file. Paint geometry only. (2) OLD CLEANS COUNT EVERY FINISHED STREET (parts/app3.js, cleanWorkM): a clean archived before stored work totals existed derives its metres per street, and a street recorded as finished WITH NO TIME (a sanctioned state — the snapshot writes null and the loader deliberately preserves it) that is missing from the clean's sides map fell out of the sides-branch fallback on a truthiness test, undercounting that clean's work total and every pace figure built on it by the street's full both-sides length. The fallback now tests key presence, exactly as the function's own items derivation and cleanCount already do; a street WITH a sides entry still prices by its bits, and stored workM is still read verbatim. (3) THE HEADING MATH SURVIVES ACCUMULATION (parts/app3.js, headDelta): the smoothed heading accumulates in updateHeading and is never normalized; the old wrap formula is correct only while the difference stays above −540°, so after roughly a lap and a half of net same-direction circling in one session — courts and crescents are normal plowing — a single GPS fix could swing the smoothed heading by −112° against a true +38.6° step and jerk the map. The replacement formula is correct over a ±2000° sweep and byte-identical to the old one on every input where the old one worked; the GPS pipeline itself is otherwise untouched. (4) A ROUTE ID CANNOT BE RE-MINTED OVER A HIDDEN PACK (parts/create-packs.js, uniqueRouteId): ids were checked only against the registered pack list, but a stored user pack that fails normalization (a corrupt or older-format record, e.g. after downgrade-then-upgrade cycles) sits in the user-pack cache without ever registering — its id could be handed to a newly created route, whose save would then overwrite the stored route's record. The minting check now also consults the cache, with the prototype-safe hasOwnProperty form (a route can literally slug to 'constructor'). The guard only ever prevents an overwrite; it cannot cause one. (5) THE GATE KEEPS ITS OWN DEFAULT (scripts/select-tier.py): the tier map's rule is that an app path no rule recognizes gets the FULL battery, but a change mixing a family file with an unrecognized file classified as the family's Tier B — the unrecognized surface escaped the battery entirely. The selector now checks every app file against all known paths (families, Tier A paths and suffixes) before the family branch and sends any mix containing an unknown file to the map default; proven across the case shapes, no classification selects fewer drives than before — the gate only ever gets stricter. TESTS (Andrew's condition: the invisible bugs get permanent checks): tests/browser/selftest-pr23-fixes.mjs extracts the shipped functions — and, for the crossing scan, the shipped scan block verbatim — and proves all four JavaScript fixes in plain Node (the assessment's own numbers: the legacy clean prices 1400, not 1000; headDelta(691.4°, 10°) is +38.6°, in range; the circling recurrence never leaves ±180°; cache-held and double-collided ids suffix correctly; a crossing street listed after the street's own name is seen), and scripts/select-tier-fixtures/run-fixtures.py pins the selector's nine case shapes; BOTH suites fail on the pre-fix tree at exactly the discriminating cases (8 JS cases, 2 selector cases) and pass with the fixes, and both run in build-and-test on every tier from this release on. The version bump rides this PR because these are fixes for bugs Andrew has seen and adjudicated. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 9db9654ed34a (the Beta 1.184 build) exactly. Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.184. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.185 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.185.

## Beta 1.184 — 2026-11-13
THE STREETS AND PACE MENUS ARE REDESIGNED (Andrew, 2026-11-12: the approved frames from his design samples — Streets keeps the Nike Run Club Activity layout with REAL Mains | Secondaries tabs and a locate pin in the street name row; Pace takes the adidas Running layout — This run stat grid, verdict banner, Progress by group, Saved cleans — with sentence-case section headings; both sheets ride the existing detents: open at half screen, swipe up for full, down to half, down again to close). Presentation only: every figure binds to the same live state the old menus read, and every function (pin locate, note editor with autosave, cancel start, saved-clean open/delete) keeps its existing behaviour. (1) STREETS: a fixed head reads the route name with its state (started/not started), a hero of streets completed (count of the complete streets over the route total) over a gradient progress bar whose width is the same work fraction the dock pill reads, with road-km captions in ROAD kilometres (side-metres / 2000 — the sides come in pairs; on Route 5W the total reads 17.6 km, the real computed figure, never the sample's 18.0). The group tabs become a segmented pill on one grey track, the selected tab wearing the app gradient; the heading above the list names the group and its count. Rows: state dot at 9px in the app's state colours (gold main-pending, rose secondary-pending, mint done), street name, the locate pin immediately right of the name (bare glyph in the app link blue; its tap does exactly what it always did — centre the street and close the sheet), a note icon in the name row only while the street carries note text, the street's length (or its note text) on the quiet line as before, and the status on the right: a completed street keeps the list's full historical string ('done <when> (<ago>)', check-flag prefix included); where the old list showed nothing, the redesign's words apply — 'completed' for a completion with no recorded time, 'one side done' for a street with some sides done (a new streetHalf predicate, shared with Pace's Progress-by-group half credit; the half dot is a split disc, mint | the group's pending colour), an em dash when untouched. The COMPLETED pill retires — its one piece of information (fully done) is carried by the status slot, the mint dot and the green name together; the decision is recorded here rather than made silently. The inline note editor gains the design's label ('Note — <street>') and explicit Save note / Done actions wired to the same autosave mechanism (Save flushes the same setNoteText call immediately; Done closes the editor). (2) PACE: the head reads 'This run' with the route name and started time (or not started). The six stat cells — Elapsed (a colon duration while a run is live, a dash when not), Road cleared (work in road km), ETA (the done-at clock while a live rate exists — paceInfo gains an additive etaClock field, etaTxt untouched; a dash otherwise), Streets (complete count over total), Last clean and Last pace /km (both from the MOST RECENT saved clean, not the reference clean; pace is per ROAD km — elapsed over road kilometres) — sit in the design grid with the approved line glyphs in the app blue. The verdict banner renders only when a live rate AND a reference rate both exist (projected full-clean durations compared: Faster/Slower by N min, or on pace), and otherwise the slot carries the reference line ('Reference clean (date) — total · pace /km') or the no-reference line — an honest not-started state never shows a verdict. Progress by group: complete counts 1, any-side-done counts a half, dashed halfway marker. Saved cleans are always expanded (Andrew: 'I don't want to scroll that much' was about the Streets groups; the cleans list lives in the sheet's one scroll region): date and street count left, colon duration right; a legacy clean with no recorded times is still listed ('time not recorded') and still opens and deletes, as before. Cancel start keeps its seat directly under the banner. (3) THE CLEAN DETAIL MOVES INTO THE PACE SHEET (it lived in the Streets sheet): tapping a saved clean swaps the Pace content for the detail in place — back link '‹ Saved cleans', the date with street count and finish clock, four stat cells (Total time = the saved-cleans row's interval, Road cleared, Pace /km, GPS trail km — the trail is travel, road cleared is work; they stay separate figures with separate labels), the kept-verbatim honesty note about both intervals, the streets with their times and spans exactly as before, NO pins anywhere in the detail, a note icon only on a street that had a note saved on that clean with the note readable read-only on tap, the noted-but-not-cleaned section with group pending dots, and Delete this clean as the design's centred red text action with the same two-tap confirm. The shell is untouched: grab, heads, the shared stops, the detach/swipe/close machinery and its animation tokens are the 1.79–1.83 code — the redesign fills the existing frame. Type is the app font stack throughout, upright (no italic, no condensed anywhere in the new CSS); blues are the app's own (gradient #3D65EF→#1B2FB6, link #2C4AD2, lightened #8FA8FF at night for the blue-on-dark slots). TESTS: drive192 (new) proves the redesign in the rendered page in both themes — detent resting heights for both sheets at three viewports (open at half, swipe up to full, down to half, down to closed), the tab swap (6 mains / 28 secondaries, no cross-group rows), pin tap centring the map on the street's middle point with the sheet closed, row tap opening the note editor with its label and Save/Done actions, the note icon present only where a note exists, the honest not-started Pace values, the verdict gating, Progress-by-group half credit, the clean detail opening inside the Pace sheet with its GPS trail figure distinct from road cleared, note icons only on noted streets, no pin in the detail, and the two-tap delete. Re-derived at equal strength: drive145 N32/N33 (the old Pace natural-height assertions measured the retired compact content; the new content is tall by design and the half stop is the measured contract now), drive88 (the COMPLETED pill assertions become status-slot assertions at the same strength), drive187/188 (the clean detail's new home in the Pace sheet; Saved cleans always expanded — the histHead toggle assertions retire with the toggle), drive89 (the detail's sheet topology re-derived for the Pace sheet at Tier C, where it gates). GATE FIXES ON THIS PR: (i) the foreign-write census pins were regenerated in this change (totals UNCHANGED, 53 expressions / 21 lines, verified pair-for-pair against the recomputed diff: every app3 site moved +71 lines on the renderPace growth, no foreign write added or removed — the round's new writes are to function locals or to a part's own bindings); (ii) Codex review (PR #22): the Pace header now takes the route name as text, never markup — a created or restored route's name is user-controlled, and concatenated into innerHTML it could mint elements inside the sheet (the Streets route line already used textContent for the same reason); (iii) Codex's second-round finding was REAL and is FIXED: the redesign's pinned Streets head sits outside #sheetBody, so at the half stop the scroll region's top can fall below the software keyboard's edge, where ensureFieldVisible (which scrolls only inside #sheetBody) could never surface the note editor — while the note editor is open the sheet now stands at the FULL stop (the drag settle's own end state, reached through the shared transition), lifting the scroll region above the keyboard, and returns to the half stop when the editor closes (a sheet the user dragged to full himself is never lowered). drive88's K1 and drive135's 122-K3 were re-derived to the new pre-keyboard size at equal strength (K3 now captures the size live before the shrink instead of pinning the half-stop value), and drive192 gains KB1–KB3 measuring the failure property at 390×844: the raise, the scroll-region top clearing a ~300px keyboard edge with the editor inside the visible region, and the return to half. (iv) Codex's suggestion to refresh the ELAPSED cell periodically while Pace stays open was adjudicated and DECLINED for this round: every figure in the app is render-bound (the old This-run line included) and any interaction re-renders — a ticker is a behaviour addition beyond the approved design, left for a follow-up at Andrew's call. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 825c8ee2ccc7 (the Beta 1.183 build) exactly. Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.183. ## Beta 1.183 — 2026-11-12
THE STORAGE STATE IS SURFACED, AND THE OFFLINE PACK IS BUDGETED (Andrew, 2026-11-12: the cache/storage fix-all — the two builds left open by the 2026-11-10 storage audit, its §6 items 3 and 4; the audit's other cache thread, the 2026-11-02 deep audit's C01–C03 test-proof gaps, was adjudicated against this tree in the same pass and needed NO work: all three were already fixed in Beta 1.73 and verified still live). All of it lives in parts/app4.js; the service worker is untouched. (1) THE STORAGE ROW TELLS THE TRUTH (Settings → Status, HEALTH card): since Beta 1.57 the app has asked the platform to exempt its data from automatic storage eviction and THROWN THE ANSWER AWAY — the code said so itself ('fire-and-forget — the answer changes nothing in the UI'). The boot request's answer is now RECORDED (storagePersist) and the row reads it beside the existing scratch-write probe: a grant paints ok ('protected · 84 MB used of ~1.2 GB'), a REFUSAL paints warn ('not protected · 84 MB used'), and an unreported answer paints idle ('not reported by this browser') — never green, per the row family's honesty rule. The usage and quota figures are measured live at pane open by navigator.storage.estimate(), guarded both ways: absent on older engines or rejecting, the row degrades to its unmeasured form — it never throws and never invents a figure. Nothing about the readout is stored; like every other Status row it is re-measured each time the pane opens. (2) THE PACK IS WEIGHED (same pane, MAPS card): the pack census (packState) used to COUNT the offline pack ('897 files') and never weigh it — no byte measure existed anywhere. The census now sums every stored entry's blob size in the same pass, and the maps sub-line carries it: '897 files · 24 MB · Route area'. The census's only caller is renderStatus, so the ~897 reads are a sub-second one-off at pane open, never on boot; a census that cannot run leaves the figure honestly absent (the preload-record fallback carries counts only), never estimated. (3) THE PACK IS CAPPED AT ITS ONLY DOOR: OFFLINE_PACK_CAP_BYTES = 250 MB, one named constant beside the preload (Andrew's number, 2026-11-12: ~3–10× above any legitimate pack — the 897-file manifest measures in the tens of MB — so it never bites a real download, while still catching the audit's named failure: the union manifest growing silently as more routes download into the one shared pack cache until the origin's quota wall is hit unwarned). Enforcement is mechanical, at the pack's single write path (the page's preload — the worker only reads the pack): the download first weighs what is already stored, per URL, so a re-download replaces a file's weight instead of double-counting it and added routes count against the same budget; every further write is weighed BEFORE it lands, and a file that would cross the cap is refused, stops the workers, and is reported in the completion line's own idiom — 'saved X of Y files — stopped at the 250 MB storage limit'. What is already stored stays stored: a partial pack is a supported, disclosed state and Status renders it 'Partial' with its measured bytes. A file whose size cannot be read is stored unweighed rather than lost. TESTS: drive161 gains T2a–T2e — the row's measured natural state in the rig (a real persist refusal: warn, with a measured usage figure), the sub-line's byte figure checked against packState's own return, the grant/idle forms driven through the recorded answer, and a rejecting estimate() degrading the row without throwing (T2a's first-run failure was the TEST's own: an undoubled regex backslash inside the ev template literal, the app row reading exactly as designed — recorded on the check). The cap itself is proven in the offline rig as E25 (tests/browser/e2e-offline.mjs): a variant page built with a 512 KB cap (the E11 in-place-rewrite pattern — no test-only seam in shipped code) must stop its download at the cap, name the limit in the completion line, leave the stored pack at or under the cap, and read Partial with measured bytes in Status; the e2e gate runs at Tier C/nightly per the tier map. The foreign-writes census totals are UNCHANGED (53 expressions / 21 lines): every new write is to a function local or to app4's OWN new bindings (OFFLINE_PACK_CAP_BYTES, fmtStoreSize, storagePersist); the app4 reset-block pin moved on line count alone and the census was regenerated in the same act, per the standing discipline. GATE FIXES ON THIS PR (Codex review, both findings verified against the code and fixed): (i) the cap check now RESERVES a file's bytes in packBytes synchronously, before its write is awaited - the first form updated the total only after each awaited put, so the five concurrent workers could all pass the check against the same stale total and overshoot the cap; a failed write now releases its reservation, and the census still passes exactly (the reservation writes are to the same function locals). (ii) E25 restamps the variant page's CSP script hash after rewriting the cap literal - the literal sits inside the stamped inline script, so the unrestamped variant's own policy blocked its script and the check could never go live (this, not the local rig, was E25's local failure). (iii) The budget now binds the pack's SECOND write path as well (Codex's follow-up on the fix head): migratePack - the boot migration/heal that copies missing manifest responses from the browsing cache into the pack - weighs the pack first and counts a copy that would cross the cap as failed, so the completion flag stays unset and the next boot retries, exactly like a failed copy; the scoping pass's 'one write path' claim was wrong and is corrected here. E25 gains E25c proving the healed pack stays at or under the cap (46 e2e checks). (iv) The two writers now TAKE TURNS (Codex's second follow-up): the boot heal fires un-awaited and the download is never disabled during it, so two writers could hold independent pack snapshots and together overshoot - a shared promise-chain turn (takePackWriteTurn) now serializes them, each censusing the pack when its own turn starts and releasing in its finally, so the budget is genuinely shared; the chain is page-memory only and dies with the page. (v) A shrinking replacement now frees its bytes only after its write succeeds (Codex's third follow-up): the reservation form applied a negative size delta immediately, so another worker could spend the freed headroom and a failed shrink then silently left the pack over the cap with no cap stop reported; growth still reserves before the await, a shrink reserves nothing. (vi) The writers' turn is now a WEB LOCK where the platform offers one (Codex's fourth follow-up): Cache Storage is origin-wide, so two tabs each holding only a per-page chain could still spend the same headroom; takePackWriteTurn takes the origin-wide 'snowroute-pack-write' Web Lock (released by the platform if a tab dies), falling back to the in-page chain on older engines, where the budget is shared within the page only - stated in the code comment, not hidden. (vii) migratePack's non-manifest prune now runs BEFORE its census and copy loop (Codex's fifth follow-up): counted toward the heal budget, obsolete entries could refuse every copy and return before the prune ever ran, leaving their space unreclaimable on every subsequent boot - the freed space now counts toward the budget (the prune's status reporting is unchanged, and E19's contract - the pack ends as exactly the manifest - is preserved). ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 33d122ba96 (the Beta 1.182 build) exactly. Plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.182 (PR #20, cache fix-all S3: deliberate Firebase cache headers in the manual deploy workflow — config-only, no bump). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.183 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.183.

## Beta 1.182 — 2026-11-12
THREE OF ANDREW'S CALLS IN ONE PUSH (his instruction: "all this is going to be pushed as one"). (1) THE CREATE-FLOW SELECTION MATCHES THE WIDER ROADS (parts/create-pick.js): Andrew on the live 1.181 build — in Create New Route the basemap roads grew, but the coloured selection drawn on a picked street stayed the old small size. THE MECHANISM, reproduced and measured before fixing: the draft's drawn widths are FIXED constants from Beta 1.127 (casing 8.5, chosen line 4.5, unchosen 3) that never carried a zoom curve or the 1.181 factor, while the basemap road under them is 12 px at z17 since 1.181 — before 1.181 the 8.5 casing spanned the 8 px road, so the selection covered the street; after 1.181 the whole selection sat inside the road with white showing both sides (rendered pixel scan of the pristine build: chosen line 4.5 px, total selection span 8.5 px, road 12 px). THE FIX: the three DRAWN widths carry the same x1.5 (casing 12.75, chosen 6.75, unchosen 4.5 — the 1.127 chosen/unchosen ratio unchanged), as literals with the coupling stated on the lines (the factor constants live in the generator's and the engine closure's scopes, not this part's). The invisible crDraftHit pick target (24) is NOT scaled and no tap box moves: the tap geometry is Andrew's device-tuned precision (1.156), untouched — this is the drawing only. (2) BUILDINGS AT 1.5X (scripts/genstyles.mjs + regenerated parts/pmstyles.js): Andrew's pick from the 3D samples (1.5x — 2x/3x declined). The generator's add3d height expression becomes ['*', ['coalesce', ['get', 'height'], 7], 1.5] — a recorded height and the 7 m estimate scale together, the z15→15.3 ramp unchanged — and the regenerated styles are byte-verified: the ONLY deltas against the 1.181 styles are the two buildings-3d height expressions (day and night), so 1.181's ROAD_WIDTH_K road widths survive regeneration untouched. (3) THE GPS PUCK (parts/app3.js + parts/app2.js): the flat 1.49 dot and its shadow are retired; pushGps now emits two ground-plane discs and addGpsLayers draws them as fill-extrusions standing on the same base — a WHITE disc (r 16 m) at 3.5 m and the BLUE disc (r 11.5 m) at 5 m — one stepped object: from the side a white band with blue above it, from above the familiar blue dot inside its white rim (the Apple Maps marker). Andrew's correction to the samples is the design: rim and blue rise TOGETHER — never a 3D blue standing on a flat white ring. The heading beam (already in-plane), the accuracy ring and the ENTIRE GPS pipeline (estimator, dispPos smoothing, watch logic) are untouched — rendering only; the trail line's under-the-stack anchor now names the stack's bottom layer (gps-beam) since gps-shadow is gone. The radii are ground metres, so the screen read is CLAMPED across the map's z8-19 (Codex review on this PR — verified against the code: unclamped, the 32 m disc would read ~2 px at z12 and ~297 px at z19, where the flat dot read a constant ~27 px): the white radius keeps its 16 m ground size wherever that projects inside 14-110 px across and freezes at the band edge outside it, the scale measured off the map's own projection at the puck as the WORST-DIRECTION stretch over both ground axes, from centred probes re-measured over ~16 px of ground so the reading stays local (a 100 m probe averages the perspective gradient and under-reads diagonally at max zoom under pitch) (an x-only measure diverges as the heading-up camera rotates toward an east/west course — Codex P1 on this PR: at bearing 89 the x-only puck read ~802 px; and any single axis still leaks under the 60-degree pitch, where the probed axis can be the foreshortened one — Codex's third catch), and the render key carries every camera input the puck's projection depends on — quarter-zoom, half-degree-bearing, 2-degree-pitch, the puck's projected position on a 16 px grid, and the viewport size (a pitched PAN with a stationary fix moves the puck through the perspective gradient without changing zoom, bearing or pitch — Codex's fourth catch) — so the glyph re-pushes whenever anything that could resize it changes. TESTS: the foreign-writes census passes EXACTLY (53 expressions / 21 lines — no pinned site moved); the rendering contract's drives are re-derived at the same strength, never weakened — drive124 (the marker renders at the fix, survives marking and a basemap restack: now the puck layers), drive125 (theme-swap snapshot's gps presence: gps-puck-blue), drive129 (beam discipline: kinds carry puckblue; C5 now pins the shadow's RETIREMENT alongside the puck's presence), drive133 (the live marker in view-only mode: puckblue). Behavioural assertions — position, smoothing, accuracy, beam discipline — are unchanged everywhere. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main 949f882a1b (the Beta 1.181 build) exactly. Plus 0 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.181. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.182 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.182.

## Beta 1.181 — 2026-11-12
EVERY ROAD IS 1.5X WIDER (Andrew, 2026-11-12 — picked from the road-width samples: "I like the 1.5x… make sure all the roads get enlarged not just my route… the whole map every road"). ONE FACTOR, THREE WORLDS, because a paint-only widening is measurably wrong here (the samples show it: at 1.5x paint-only, a finished side's green line ends up buried inside the widened surface with red paint outside it). THE PAINT (parts/engine.js): WIDTH_K = 1.5 scales the surface, band and band-casing curves at definition, the SEPARATE inline centre-casing curve in the stack, the selection glow on BOTH of its paths (the initial stack's wPlus deltas and the live applyGlow/glowW path — patching only the first leaves the on-screen glow unscaled), the selection core (proportional via wScale), and the two fixed 3 px edge lines. THE GEOMETRY: the finished-side lines are real offset polylines, not paint offsets, so the metre-world scales by the same K — the ±3.3 m offset distance in buildEngineGeometry, SURF_HALF/BAND_HALF, and the junction band threshold all ×1.5, so edges ride the surface edge and junction end-trims still butt correctly; offsets are derived at route load and never stored, so no saved data, backup or storage format changes shape. THE TAPS (parts/app2.js): the centreline tap tolerance goes 16 → 24 px (16 × K) so a tap anywhere on the wider paint still selects instead of deselecting; the dormant hit layer scales to 24K for truthfulness. The create flow's own pick geometry is a separate, device-tuned system and is deliberately NOT coupled (verified end-to-end against the wider basemap). THE BASEMAP: no runtime override — scripts/genstyles.mjs gains a generation-time transform (ROAD_WIDTH_K = 1.5, stamped in the generated banner) scaling every line-width and line-gap-width output on roads* line layers ×1.5 in BOTH flavours (35 + 17 expressions per flavour, verified exact against the unscaled styles; label halo widths untouched), and parts/pmstyles.js is regenerated through the normal pipeline (npm ci; regeneration byte-identical). DAY AND NIGHT ARE ONE WIDTH: syncTheme never carried a width, and both flavours bake the same factor — proven in the rendered app by a day→night→day toggle measurement in this round's QA (route casing/surface and a basemap road read exactly 1.5x after each restyle). FIXED WIDTH, NO SETTINGS TOGGLE (Andrew's pick of the audit's clean route). TESTS: the Carbon sweep's B-07 proof re-derives its three SURF_HALF clip expectations at 4.95 m (3.3 × K) and passes; the foreign-writes census passes UNCHANGED (the pinned sites live in files this change does not shift); no drive asserts a road width, so no drive is rewritten. ROLLBACK: this release is one PR — reverting its merge commit restores main b540d7960b (the Beta 1.180 build) exactly; a snapshot of the live 1.180 build is kept with this round's record. Plus 1 maintenance PR since Beta 1.180 (PR #17, generic repo-description docs). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.181 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.181.

## Beta 1.180 — 2026-11-12
THE SPLASH IS ICON ONLY (Andrew, 2026-11-12: "take out the text… keep the icon dead center in the middle and that's it… keep the whole process the same"). THE CHANGE: the #splashTitle element and its slide-in CSS are removed from the splash; the existing plow icon is the brand block's only child, and #brandLayer's existing centred flex layout now places the icon's own 168px box at the geometric centre of the splash viewport. No other screen changes: the app-name strings in the page title, manifest and menu header are untouched. THE PROCESS IS THE SAME: the blue and snow are unchanged, the icon keeps its own .35s fade-in at 1.25s and its size and artwork, the boot gate keeps its 2700ms animated / 1900ms reduced-motion floor, and Beta 1.178's ending stands — at splash completion the brand layer cuts instantly to display:none and the Choose/Create pills fade in over .35s on a solo doors menu. BOOT TRIGGER: the boot tail in parts/app4.js no longer listens for the retired title slide's animationend. It listens for the icon fade's animationend instead; because that animation ends at 1.6s rather than the title slide's 2.15s, the post-event breath is recalibrated from 150ms to 700ms so the tail still lands at the same ~2.3s point, with the 2.6s timer remaining a fallback only. TESTS: drive132 re-pins S1 to title-absent + the icon's rendered centre and S5 to the event-gated boot window (not the fallback); drive180 is re-derived around the icon as the sole brand subject — its dark-window, arrival, hold-centre, cut and solo-menu properties are measured on the icon itself, while the title-only GAP/SEQ3 checks retire with the title. Plus 2 maintenance PRs since Beta 1.179 (PR #14, gate hardening; PR #15, standing version-rule docs). ## Beta 1.178 — 2026-11-12
THE DOORS MENU STANDS SOLO (Andrew, 2026-11-12: he asked to change the intro again — "I want the second menu with our choose route and create route to be solo again. So after the splash screen animations the icon and title text should just disappear for the transition to the next menu" — reviewed three transition feels on a demo page (A Cut / B Quick fade / C Lift away) and picked A). THE CUT: the splash is byte-for-byte the show it has been — blue, falling snow, the icon fading in at 1.25 s, the title sliding in at 1.5 s, the hold to the boot gate's floor (2700 ms animated / 1900 ms reduced-motion) — and at the moment the gate arms the menu, the icon + SNOW ROUTE title DISAPPEAR INSTANTLY: arm() (parts/create-save.js) adds one class, .cut, and #brandLayer.cut is display:none. No fade, no drift, no scale, no travel — the brand is at full paint in the hold and absent from the very next frame. THE MENU SOLO, IN EVERY STATE: .cut is never removed within a session, so the main pane, every sub-pane, the boot-create park and the return to the picker (unloadRouteToBoot still clears .off/.bye; .cut stands) all show the doors with no icon and no title; a fresh load replays the splash and cuts again at the same moment. THE PILLS' ARRIVAL: with nothing left to clear their path, the Choose/Create pills simply fade in — the boot pane rule (parts/head.html) is now a plain .35 s opacity fade, no delay, and the 14 px lowered start the panes rose from since 1.153 is retired with it. RETIRED MACHINERY, REMOVED NOT PARKED: the 1.162–1.164 FLIP (arm()'s measure-invert-play flight of #brandBlock, 1.3 s cubic-bezier(.65,0,.35,1)) and its landing pose rules in head.html (#brandLayer.menu and the calc(35.49% - 209.42px) halved-gap pose — the 1.157 slot lineage through 1.158/1.159/1.163 with it); and the 1.165 pill-wait gate entire — rmMain's .hold/.go classes, their two CSS rules, arm()'s rAF clearance watch with its transitionend and 1500 ms fallbacks, and finish()'s strip of the classes at a pick. THE SNOW DOES NOT MOVE: the canvas lives in #splash, not in the brand layer, and its engine keys the scene off the layer's .bye/.off classes, never .cut — so the falling snow and the settling bank run on the solo menu exactly as they did under the landed brand, until a pick ends the scene. The layer's other lifetime machinery is untouched (.bye at a pick, .off/.parked parking, .away on sub-panes — the last three inert under .cut, as recorded at the .cut rule). REDUCED MOTION: the same cut on the same 1900 ms floor — an instant disappearance is this transition's motion-safe form; nothing animates at the handoff in any mode now. TESTS: drive180 is re-derived around the cut, no check silently weakened — its SEQ checks (the dark window, the two arrivals) and S1/S2/GAP/SIZE-of-the-hold stand; the flight family (F0, N1–N4, BLOCK1–3, DURATION, S3, S5, SLOT, CLEAR) retires WITH the flight it judged and is replaced by the CUT family measuring the new failure properties directly: no transform transition ever runs on the block, no painted brand frame exists after the flip, no partial-opacity fade frame exists in between, and at rest the layer computes display:none under .cut with no inline transform residue; the TITLE family inverts (the title is now absent on the main pane too, and never returns across the pane round trips) while SNOW6 keeps the snow's sub-pane lifetime, and a new SOLO check measures both pills fully in bounds on the brand-free menu. README counts re-derived by scripts/release.py for the changed check roster. Gated per the tier map — this change alters entrance JS behaviour in parts/create-save.js, so it rides the PR gate at the tier the map assigns (not the Tier-1 direct-push lane). THE LAUNCH STILLS DO NOT CHANGE — measured, not assumed: all twelve apple-touch-startup-image files' pixels sit entirely on the blue gradient ramp (every channel's extrema are exactly the gradient's two stops), no mark is embedded in them, so there is nothing to regenerate. THE SERVICE WORKER DOES NOT CHANGE: it carries no icon bytes and no icon URLs (its install precaches only the two integrity-checked CDN libraries and the shell page), its cache names are the app's fixed identity and are never bumped per release, and the shell it fetches fresh on update already carries the new inlined icon — the home-screen icon on iOS stays frozen at install time regardless, as established in Beta 1.171 (delete/re-add, Backup export first). NOTHING ELSE MOVES: no app logic, no behaviour, no other asset. Gated per the tier map (Tier B — the session family for the What's New surface; the Tier A core set also run for the head surface this change touches).
