## Beta 1.176 — 2026-11-11
SLOW LOADS ONLY: THE PROGRESS PILL WAITS FIVE SECONDS (Andrew, 2026-11-11, on his phone screenshot of the create flow: tapping a city flashed 'Loading Vaughan — part 0 of 30 · 0 s' with a Cancel button for a fraction of a second — 'it happens so quick that it just looks like a glitch'). THE GATE (parts/create-pick.js, crRenderHint — the pick-stage hint's one writer): both in-progress load readouts — the city grid's and the fallback box's — now render only once the load has been running 5 s (CR_LOAD_GATE_MS, read off the load's own startedAt, so a readout that appears carries the load's true progress at that moment; the loads' own 1 s tickers re-render through the same writer as the gate opens, so no second timer exists to go stale, and a load that settles earlier never renders the readout at all). Inside the gate the pill paints NOTHING — no readout, no Cancel, and no disclosure note leaking it open early (the cache/pack notes are state, and ride the first ungated render or the terminal state instead). Resuming a stopped load restarts its own clock (crGridResume already restarts startedAt). THE READOUT'S NEW FORMAT (Andrew's amendment, minutes later: 'Don't use zero out of 30. Just use a percentage'): a city load past the gate reads exactly 'Loading <City> — <pct>%' — cells done of cells total as a rounded percentage, the same honest counters the retired readout used, in the form he asked for. Gone with the old format: the part counts, the elapsed-seconds suffix, and the failed-so-far tally (failures surface in the degraded/failed states). The pack path's separate wording ('Loading <City> from the app's own library…', Beta 1.123) retires with it — one readout format for a city load, and a pack's cells are the same counters. THE FALLBACK BOX keeps its truthful wording past the gate ('Preparing street shapes for <City>…') minus the seconds suffix: the box is ONE fetch with no countable parts, so no percentage exists for it and an invented one would be a fabrication, not a format. THE CANCEL BUTTON'S PLACEMENT (his screenshot again: Cancel sat ON the pill's bottom edge): through 1.175 the button's top was a fixed CSS offset tuned to a one-line pill, so any pill that wrapped to two lines grew down over the button. The writer now places the button from the pill's RENDERED bottom edge at every render — pill shown: button top = pill bottom + 8 px; pill hidden: the CSS offset stands, so the button's pill-less perch (an outstanding offer in the ready state) is unchanged. EVERY LOAD NOW ENDS IN THE CONFIRMATION (Andrew: 'if it successfully loaded then just put load successful type thing like we had before'): a fresh (non-cached) city load — silent in the ready state since 1.175 — now shows '<City> — loaded' on success, riding the same Beta 1.153 4 s self-dismissal clock (the confirmation key extends to fresh loads, keyed by the load's own start, so a resumed load's success confirms as its own event and a dismissed one is never resurrected by re-renders — drive178 pins both the cached and the fresh forms). The cached form ('<City> — loaded <date>') is unchanged. NEVER GATED: the failure state with its Retry / 'Prepare this area' remedy and the degraded state with Resume / 'Retry missing parts' appear the moment a load resolves badly, however fast that is (drive178 P1a/P1b, drive152 K2 re-verified; K2 gains a placement measurement). THE PERCENTAGE IS NOW A STANDING RULE, APPLIED APP-WIDE IN THIS SAME RELEASE (Andrew's second amendment: 'anything that says loading, always make it a percentage') — every loading/downloading progress readout in the app was enumerated and each is converted, exempt, or named out of scope: CONVERTED — the create grid's cancelled terminal state ('Loading stopped — <City> <pct>% loaded', the landed share of its cells; Resume unchanged) and the offline-maps download readout in Settings (parts/app4.js: 'Downloading… <pct>%', the same fraction its progress bar already painted — through 1.175 the line showed the file count while the bar beside it showed the percentage; the display sweep that eases the shown value toward the true one is untouched, so the percentage sweeps as the count did). EXEMPT, because no measurable progress exists for them (a percentage would be invented): 'Loading cities…' (the catalogue pane's row while the city library loads), 'Backup restored — reloading…', 'New version found — reloading…', and the fallback box's readout (one fetch, no countable parts — gated, seconds suffix dropped, truthful wording kept). KEPT AS THEY ARE, because they are not loading readouts: the download job's completion line and toast ('saved X of Y files' — an outcome report of a finished job), the Status page's offline-maps census ('X of Y files' — a stored-state census, not a load in progress), the degraded pill's missing-parts disclosure (the count there is the failure detail itself, which the terminal notices exist to carry), the run counter (streets done already reads 'N of M · <pct>%'), and every non-loading count the rule does not govern (sectors picked, sides done, stretch position). TESTS, re-derived with disclosure (no check silently weakened): drive166 F1 pinned the old part-count readout mid-flight — it now pins the gate itself (2.5 s into a doomed load: no readout, no Cancel) and gains F1b (the same load past the gate, clock seeded: the percentage format + Cancel, placed clear of the pill); drive166 J2a likewise splits into the gate pin and J2b (seeded past the gate: 'Loading Vaughan — 0%', Cancel clear at 390 and 430 px widths, measured from the rendered boxes); drive153 A3 pinned the pack readout naming the library during the wait — superseded by the amendment: it now pins the gate during the wait and A3b the percentage form past it, the wire facts (zero Overpass requests, one pack download, fromPack) untouched; drive152 K2c and drive178 P1a's new companion pin the button's placement by measurement in the degraded and failed states (long wording that wraps — the overlap's worst case); drive178 P1d/P1e pin the fresh-load confirmation and its self-dismissal. Placement is proven BY MEASUREMENT everywhere it is pinned: bounding boxes in the rendered page, gap > 0, zero intersection, at 390 and 430 px widths (the two elements' CSS carries no theme variant - their geometry is defined once, so the measurement is the geometry in both themes, and the placement is recomputed from the live box at every render in any case). Gated per the tier map (create family + the version/What's New surfaces). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.176 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.176.

## Beta 1.175 — 2026-11-11
THE CREATE MAP KEEPS ONE PILL (Andrew, 2026-11-11: the only popup under the search bar should be the initial load confirmation — the city-loaded note that stays three to four seconds and disappears; the other hints are unnecessary). THE REMOVAL (parts/create-pick.js, crRenderHint — the pick-stage hint's one writer): the ready state's two informational texts are gone — 'Tap a road on the map to add it' (shown once the draft held a street) and 'Street shapes ready — tap a road' (shown before the first pick). A ready city that did not come from the on-device cache now renders NO pill text of its own: no replacement text, and the writer's visibility toggle now requires text, so the state never paints an empty pill either. WHAT STAYS, state by state, because none of it is an informational popup: the cached load confirmation itself ('<City> — loaded <date>'), still on the Beta 1.153 self-dismissal clock — verified in the code before anything moved: crRenderHint arms a 4000 ms timer for exactly this one render state, a re-render of the same confirmation leaves the clock running, road taps never resurrect a dismissed one, and failures, progress, offers and disclosures never ride the clock (drive178 P2a/P2b/P3c pin all of it and are untouched); the failure state with its working Retry / 'Prepare this area' remedy; the degraded state's Resume / 'Retry missing parts'; both preparing progress readouts with Cancel; an outstanding offer's button (crWanted) in every state that carries it; the sector-trimming state's instruction while a trimming session is open; the no-grid guidance state ('Search for a street in…', with its 'Prepare this area' button at zoom); the transient 'Checking the whole of…' progress line during a tap verification; and both disclosure notes — the cache note and the pack note — which ride the hint in every state. ONE HYGIENE CONSEQUENCE, handled in the same change: with the ready-state texts gone, a disclosure note can now ride an otherwise empty hint (a fresh grid load whose pack fell back to the service, a cache save refused under the storage cap), so the two note appends stand the note alone instead of leaving a dangling ' · ' separator — in every state that already had text the append is byte-identical to before (drive153 C1's fallback disclosure and drive166 J11b's cache-cap disclosure re-verified against the new form). DELIBERATELY LEFT: the event toast on an out-of-coverage tap ('Street shapes aren't ready for this area yet — tap Prepare this area, then tap … again', create-pick.js) is a different surface carrying an actionable remedy, not the status pill, and is untouched, as is its companion 'Still preparing…' toast. TESTS: no drive pinned either removed text (whole-tree grep: the literals lived only in parts/ and the assembled outputs); every hint assertion in the create family grades a kept state — progress, failure, degraded, the cached confirmation and its clock, the two disclosures — so no check is re-derived or removed this round. Gated per the tier map (create family + the version/What's New surfaces). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.175 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.175.

## Beta 1.174 — 2026-11-11
THE BLUE UNIFIES ON THE ICON (Andrew's call, 2026-11-11, made on the both-directions samples: the current icon's shaded blue is nicer, so the menus and everything wearing the flat blue match the icon). Through 1.173 the app ran two blues: the icon's field is a diagonal shaded gradient — (61,101,239) at the top-left deepening to (27,47,182) at the bottom-right, from the icon's own recipe — while every menu, button and paint surface wore the flat #2b4bf2 the Beta 1.100-era unification had settled on. This release retires the flat blue as a live colour. LARGE SURFACES (parts/head.html: the boot root frame html.boot / html.boot #map, #splashBg, #routeMenu, #setMenu) and FILLED BUTTONS (#hud button, .banner button, #startBtn/#resetBtn — Start and Finish, #filters button.on, #noteYes, #crSearchBtn, #crHintBtn, .crPrimary, #crReviewBtn) now wear the icon's diagonal as a CSS gradient: linear-gradient(135deg, #3d65ef, #1b2fb6). FLAT-ONLY contexts take the gradient family's midpoint #2c4ad2 (the gradient's own centre: (44,74,210)): the theme-color meta, --link in both themes, .tabItem.on in both themes, the route badge's ink, the manifest's background_color, and the map paint family in parts/app2.js (gps-ring, gps-ring-line, gps-dot, trail-line — and the GPS facing beam's fan, the same family's canvas paint, its hue remapped to rgba(44,74,210,...) with its alpha fade unchanged); the --chip tint hue follows to rgba(44,74,210,.06) by day and rgba(44,74,210,.10) by night, alphas untouched. THE LAUNCH STILLS: the twelve apple-touch-startup-image files are regenerated (gen_launch.py's plain mode now paints the diagonal, CSS-135deg geometry) wearing the same gradient at their existing dimensions, so the phone's first paint matches the new splash — and the boot root frame is the gradient from the first frame, so no frame can flash the retired flat blue. ONE MECHANISM NOTE: the snow scene's transparency rule (body.snow-scene #routeMenu.boot) now clears the whole background shorthand, not background-color alone — the menu's blue is a background-image now, and clearing only the colour would have left the gradient painted under the snow. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: no geometry, no non-blue colour (the primary pill's deep-blue ink #173a8a is a different, deliberate blue and stays), the basemap's own paint and the blue-grey secondary tones stay, the icon files themselves are untouched (they were already the shaded original), and the snow engine is untouched (the blue behind the bank comes from the page background this release repaints). The old value survives only as history — in this changelog's older entries and in the 1.132 note, reworded so it no longer reads as current. Checks re-derived, not weakened: drive125 S1/T3, drive132 S1 and drive89 T1 pinned the flat blue's computed background-color; they now pin the gradient itself (the computed background-image carries both stops) and drive132 pins the theme-color at the midpoint. Gated per the tier map (the head, session and manifest paths: build-and-test + the core set and the session family). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.174 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.174.

## Beta 1.173 — 2026-11-11
THE SNOWBANK'S FADE REACHES ITS CREST (Andrew's call, 2026-11-11, made on the faithful side-by-side sample — the last held item of the Carbon audit, C-08, graded LOW / INCONSISTENCY and parked at 1.170 as out of scope until he ruled). THE DEFECT (parts/brand-snow.js, render — the boot/menu snow layer's bank paint): the settled bank's fill is a two-stop white gradient, and its anchor was a Beta 1.158 leftover — createLinearGradient(0, H - 22, 0, H), written when the settled peak stood 20 px tall. Beta 1.161 raised the peak to 58 px (groundHeightAt: maxH = 58 x amount) with the paint declared otherwise byte-identical, but the anchor never moved with it, so at full settle the top ~36 px of the bank sat above the gradient's start and clamped to its flat first stop: one solid shade from the crest down to 22 px above the floor, the whole fade squeezed into the bottom 22 px. THE FIX (one line): the gradient now anchors at the bank's actual crest for the current amount — createLinearGradient(0, H - 58 * amount, 0, H) — so the same two stops, the same colours and the same alpha behaviour spread over the bank's full depth at every point of the settle cycle, exactly as the approved sample's FIXED panel rendered it. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: the two-sine profile, the 16 s build/rest/melt cycle, the three flake layers, the sprites and the fill's globalAlpha are byte-identical; the whole-tree sweep finds the old gradient line nowhere (the 22s left in parts/app2.js are an unrelated position clamp, named and untouched). Gated per the tier map (Tier A snow path: build-and-test + the core set). Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.173 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.173.

## Beta 1.172 — 2026-11-11
SHEETS GLIDE FROM THE FIRST FRAME (Andrew's call — option (a) of the spring finding the drive156-harden round gated back, decided 2026-11-11). THE DEFECT (parts/app2.js, springSheet — the one closed-form spring under every sheet settle and every create-panel morph): the spring's clock (t0) was performance.now() captured inside the call, which usually lands mid-frame during input dispatch, while the step function is evaluated at rAF frame timestamps — frame-start times. Whenever a spring registered early enough to join the same frame's callback batch, its first step ran the closed form at t ≈ −10 ms, extrapolating backward along the curve and applying a value BEHIND the start, so the flight departed from monotonic for its first two frames: a collapse went 190 → 182 → 188 before falling (drive156-harden §6), and an expand overshot before dipping back (60 → 73 → 61). Both signatures were reproduced first-hand this round under a long-task dispatch on the real collapse/expand paths (the collapse at 190 → 181 → 188; the expand twice) before any code moved. THE FIX: t0 is taken from the first step's own rAF timestamp, so t starts at exactly 0 and every flight is the closed form from its true start. Nothing else about the spring changes — the closed form, SHEET_OMEGA, the velocity clamp, the settle thresholds and every caller's apply/finish contract are untouched, and no caller depended on the old clock (audited: the two sheet settles in app2.js, the create morph and the head-drag spring-back in create-pick.js — the morph's cross-fade progress derives from the height itself, never from elapsed time). PROOF AT THE UNIT LEVEL: under the same long-task dispatch that produced 3 rebounds in 12 recorded flights before the fix, 16 of 16 recorded flights after the fix are monotonic from the first frame. drive156 RE-PROVED, QUARANTINE LIFTED (second commit of this release): over 22 local runs of the hardened drive on the fixed build, 157-D1 and 157-D2 — the checks the quarantine names — never failed, and no recorded flight anywhere in the drive shows the rebound. The whole-drive runs were not spotless, and the two recurring failures are classified TEST/ENVIRONMENT on first-hand captures, not app defects: 158-B3, whose panel-to-sector-bar flight is starved by the sector entry's own workload on the test rig (the spring's second step lands past its settle point after a ~234 ms frame gap, so the sampler records no intermediate height; the surface never reverses, starts and lands exactly, and the check's own evidence class reads INSUFFICIENT OBSERVATION), and B5/B6, where the drive taps the panel head without waiting for the preceding morph to land and, under the same starvation, the tap lands on the sector-bar face instead (when the tap lands on the head, the collapse fires immediately; B5/B6 failed identically on the unmodified app in the harden round). drive156's entry is removed from tests/quarantine.json and the create-family marker in tests/TIER-MAP.md is lifted: the drive gates at every tier again. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.172 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.172.

THE ART: the shipped icon's own shapes, traced from the 512 file — the snowflake averaged across its twelve symmetries (the hand-drawn lumps and shadow ghosts removed, the true shapes kept), the road band and its cream dashes the original's own traced shapes, never redrawn — refilled in a true deep black (flat (6,6,9); the traced charcoal shading read faded, his word) with the band thinned about 20% from both edges (his pick of three thicknesses), rendered from masters at 1024 and 2048 the shipped 512 source never had. THE SPLASH: the inlined splash icon is icon-512.png's exact bytes at assembly time (Beta 1.133), so the new art rides into the splash by construction; the twelve launch stills stay plain splash blue (Beta 1.160 Option 3) and are untouched, as are the entrance timings, the landing pose and every splash geometry pin. ## Beta 1.170 — 2026-11-11
THE CARBON FIX ROUND, PHASE 2 — the graded PARTIALLY-supported findings that remained after Beta 1.169, each re-verified first-hand against the 1.169 tree before any code moved; two items closed as no-fix on evidence and one is recorded as gated for Andrew's call (the round's PHASE2-RECORD.md carries the per-item evidence). FIXED: A-04 — the interrupted-finish reconciliation judged the load-SANITIZED history, so a newest entry the load sanitizer had dropped (a pack edit stripped every street it named) hid its interrupted finish entirely — the run start stayed live over a finish that had in fact archived — and a rollback that did fire wrote the sanitized array back, persisting the sanitizer's strips over entries storage still held raw; loadRouteState now parses the stored history twice and reconciliation judges the RAW newest entry, and a rollback writes the raw array minus its tail (memory drops the phantom only when the sanitized tail is that same entry). A-05 — the street counter divided by totalWorkM() unguarded: a route with no work in it read "NaN%" with a NaN% meter width; the denominator is read once and guarded, and an empty route reads 0%. A-08 — the day/night override's two writes rode bare lsSet; a failed write now answers with the "Not saved — storage problem" toast like every sibling save path (the override still applies for the session; only its persistence failed). B-04 — the selection glow was built from the single anchor only, so a multi-select glowed one stretch while the selection lines painted them all; buildSelGlow now emits one feature per selected stretch (buildSel's iteration), and the pulse loop keys off the selection list. B-09 — GLP.setFont replaced only the variable future layers are built from, so a basemap restyle left live label layers on the retired font stack; the existing label layers now take the new stack in place. B-10 — an empty geometry recomputed the route-name POI to [0,0] (Null Island) from the unmoved extrema seeds; the POI keeps its last real centre when there are no streets, and assignTiers returns early on an empty label set instead of indexing lens[-1]. B-12 — buildSnap read startMs null for a live run whose streets carried no recorded stamps, so the archived clean lost its startedAt and the work/path bake keyed off it; the run start itself is now the fallback. C-02 — the Stay-awake switch was the one Settings switch outside the Beta 1.113 save-honesty contract: a failed save now reverts the value and toasts, and the wake lock syncs only when the value actually changed (the value stays session-only by design, Beta 1.112). C-03 — the pack migration's "was anything ever downloaded" test read only the active route's preload record; it now asks every route's record (anyPreloadRecord), at both the boot gate and inside migratePack. C-04 — Status labelled the UNION pack census as the active route's area; the sub-line now names the union when more than one route exists and adds the active route's own share (packState carries per-route counts), and the reset line falls back to any route's download record instead of contradicting the maps line after a route switch. C-06 — Settings' Back ran the backup pane's xMode walk from any pane; the walk now runs only while the backup pane is active. C-07 — the service worker's install swallowed a failed shell fetch and activated with no HTML cached; the shell fetch is now fatal to the install (a previous worker keeps serving; a first install retries next visit), the shell lands under both the scope and scope + 'index.html', and the navigation fallback's scope match ignores the query string. C-09 — the restore door gains a single-flight guard: restoreBackup is async, and a second tap (or a file pick landing mid-restore) could snapshot the first restore's half-written state. D-03 — Save route gains the same single-flight guard: a double-tap could register one route twice under a single id (both calls computed the id before either registered), and a late tap after exitCreate threw on the nulled draft. D-04 — "Clear all" replaced draft.roads without the two duties every other roads mutation obeys; it now bumps the geometry generation (the one mutation that skipped the cache contract — the cache's roads-identity key happened to catch this replacement, but the bump is the invariant the other mutations keep) and clears the pending rows (a cleared street could previously be re-added by the pending sweep). D-07 — the Overpass fallback branch (no readable stream) checked the size cap only after the full body had buffered, in UTF-16 code units against a byte cap; a declared Content-Length over the cap now refuses up front, and the buffered text is measured in bytes. DOCS: C-05 — OFFLINE-CONTRACT.md gains the multi-route section the code has lived by since 1.117 (the preload manifest is the union over route packs; each pack's enumeration is budgeted at PACK_TILES_MAX = 20000; the migration flag certifies whatever list is current) — the frozen v1 set, its fixture and its fingerprint are untouched. NO-FIX, on first-hand evidence: A-06 — stitchRuns/computeStretches would throw on an empty segment array, but normalizePack rejects such geometry on every validated entry path (a street with no segments, a segment under two points, a street emptied by cleaning) and the draft's writers are fed by the same cleaning, so the graded scenario has no live door; B-08 — the suggestion midpoint/Yes geometry the claim targets is the first run by construction since Beta 1.144 (STRETCH.line is run 0 and sector 0 is run 0), Phase 1's B-02 guard covers the map-null tap, and a route switch resets the banner state. GATED (no code changed): B-05 — LEFTSIDE/LEFTBIT turn out to have NO reader in the current tree (dead derivation, verified by census), so nothing mislabels on screen today; a first-hand scan of the shipped Route 5W pack also shows the midpoint rule and a length-weighted dominant-direction rule disagree on 7 of 34 streets, so any future wiring of these values is a label-definition decision for Andrew, not a silent fix. OUT OF SCOPE, untouched by design: C-08 (the snowbank gradient), settings.awake persistence, the uncapped clean history, the refuted XSS suspicions, X-02/X-03/X-06. Regression coverage: new drives 190 and 191 carry the round's page-level proofs; the service-worker install change is pinned by an added case in tests/sw.test.mjs; the remaining items are pinned by fix-check probes and the existing family drives. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.170 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.170.

## Beta 1.169 — 2026-11-11
THE CARBON FIX ROUND — every graded-real finding from the Gemini 4 Carbon audit of Beta 1.168 (main chat, 2026-11-10; graded claim-by-claim against the source, Codex re-check on the synthesis), fixed at the size grading proved. Andrew's scope call: "the high, the medium, and the low — let's do them all." HIGH: B-02 — the GPS suggestion banner's Yes button dereferenced the map unguarded, so in the map-failure world (MapLibre dead, map === null) the tap threw before selecting the street; it now rides the codebase's 1.146 C4 guard idiom and still selects (the selection chips' map-projection positioning takes the same guard — unguarded, it threw on the same tap and aborted the selection refresh half-done). D-02 — a draft imported from a file never populated draft.osmNames, so sector cutting treated the street's own OSM ways as cross-streets and cut imported streets into fragments at their own vertices; importDraft now maps every imported key to itself, as tapped streets always have. A-01 (the real mechanism only) — the note editor's blur save and street-switch flush discarded setNoteText's result, so a failed save was silent whenever the storage-warning episode had already fired; blur now answers in the hint with the debounced path's own words, and the switch flush applies the Beta 1.97 finishChangeMarked contract. D-01 — exitCreate's sector-session reset had sat inside a line comment since Beta 1.153 (dead code; enterCreate's reset fenced it) and now stands on its own line and runs. MEDIUM: B-06 — cleanWorkM's legacy branch returned a partially-recorded sides map's partial total and never reached the fuller items derivation (the RP-09 short-circuit, alive for pre-1.167 cleans); the merge is now per street — a street with a sides entry is priced by its bits, a completed street missing from the map falls back to the items derivation for that street. B-07 — one clean could read "34 streets" and "33 of 34" on adjacent surfaces because refRun and the headers counted raw item keys while histDur counted only timestamped streets; every surface now counts through one helper (cleanCount — the route's streets the clean covers, timestamp or not). D-05 (+ the X-05 corollary) — normalizePack's duplicate map was a plain object, so a first street named "constructor"/"toString" was falsely rejected as a duplicate, "__proto__" could not be stored at all, and a pending street with such a name was hidden from the pick list; the map is prototype-free now and the pending check tests own keys. D-08 — picking a city by search or the last-city shortcut mid-draft switched city with no discard prompt, stranding the old draft's streets, pending rows and area under the new city; a pick that replaces a chosen city now goes through the same askDiscard + full reset as the list door (a first pick keeps its draft, as before). A-02 — the legacy migration's parity gate read storage through lsGet, which cannot tell a thrown read from an absent key, so a transient throw could commit the migration marker with a field never copied; the pass's decision reads now go through rawRead's sentinel and an unreadable field blocks the commit for a next-boot retry. A-03 — the loadSides done-time inheritance fired only for wholly-empty piece-time maps, so a partially-recorded street undercounted side-metres in the live pace; missing stamps now inherit, recorded ones are never overwritten. D-06 — restoring a cached city rebuilt the byName index inside the per-cell loop (O(cells × ways)); it rebuilds once after the fold, the identical index. B-03 — cornerTouchSlices used `return` where it meant skip: one rejected partner candidate abandoned the street end's remaining candidates; the three rejections are `continue` now, and the landed hop remains the loop's only stop. LOW: B-11 — a second GPS suggestion overwrote the single banner and was never offered again that run; suggestions now queue and each takes the banner in turn. D-09 — #createHint could clip at narrow widths (nowrap, no max-width); it is capped at min(94vw, 560px) and wraps, centred. B-01 — engine.js's glowAnim was assigned undeclared (sloppy-mode hygiene; no strict mode added anywhere). DOCS: A-07 — STORAGE-SCHEMA.md's global-key list, route-deletion sweep, restore snapshot scope and a spliced sentence corrected against the code; D-10 — six ARCHITECTURE.md contradictions corrected (the removed Refresh affordance ×2, the bounded=1 street search, the ±12/±20 tap boxes, the panel's classes and "Current selection" title, the "Select City" pane title, the "Review selection" preview title); D-11 — a head.html comment pointed at create-save.js for the snow engine; it lives in brand-snow.js. OUT OF SCOPE, untouched by design: settings.awake persistence (session-only, Andrew's Beta 1.112 decision), the uncapped clean history (Beta 1.167 B1), the refuted XSS suspicions and X-02/X-03/X-06. Regression coverage: new drives 188 and 189 carry the round's page-level proofs (188: B-02, B-06/B-07, B-11, A-01, A-02, A-03; 189: D-02, D-05, D-08); the remaining items are pinned by fix-check probes and the existing family drives. Editorial: this entry; What's New replaced with the Beta 1.169 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.169.

## Beta 1.168 — 2026-11-10
THE STREET PANEL DROPS CLEAR OF THE DAY/NIGHT BUTTON (Andrew, on his 1.167 phone test, 2026-11-10: tapping a street opens the info panel — street name, Mark completed, the note button — too high, overlapping the day/night button: "lower that and push it without tests"). SHIPPED WITHOUT THE BATTERY at his explicit order — the same one-off override class as 1.164/1.165, recorded here and in the round's record (beta1/work/panel-lower-1168/RECORD.md); the geometry was verified by direct measurement on the assembled page with the drives' own harness, not by the drive battery, and fix-checks.json is untouched (its roll follows separately, as with 1.164/1.165). THE MECHANISM (parts/head.html only, the #selPop rule — the one place the panel's top is set): the panel is a centred fixed card, width min(342px, 92vw), and the day/night button is a 56px round dockBtn at top 78, right 12 — its box ends at y 134. Measured pre-change at 390x844 and 430x932 in every state the panel can take (single selection fresh, single selection complete, multi-select, longest street name — the box is one fixed size by the Beta 1.34 design, and all four states measured identical): the panel ran y 54 to 150.52, cutting 44px into the button at 390 wide and 24px at 430, with the button's full 56px height inside the panel's span; the panel's top edge had to travel 80px (the button's bottom, 134, minus the panel's top, 54) before the two boxes separated at all. Andrew's rule — the measured overlap plus about 10px of breathing room — sets the shift at 90px: the top calc's 54px becomes 144px, landing the panel's top exactly 10px below the button's bottom edge (10px plus the safe-area inset on a phone, which the panel's rule carries and the button's does not, so the phone clearance is larger than the measured floor). The vertical geometry is width-independent, so the one shift serves both sizes. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: the panel's width, contents, states and rise animation, the button, the landing pose, the splash and the 1.165 fade gate are untouched; the panel's bottom after the drop stands at 240.52 — over 600px clear of the screen bottom at 844 — so nothing at the bottom is crowded. Editorial: this entry; What's New rolled to the single Beta 1.168 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.168.

## Beta 1.167 — 2026-11-10
RUN/PACE FIX ROUND (the fixes out of the morning's run/pace Codex audit, approved by Andrew the same day: "Approve B1–B5 (uncap in place, both durations labelled)"). FIVE ITEMS. (B1) THE 10-CLEAN CAP IS GONE: through 1.166 an archive held at most 10 cleans per route, enforced at four points — trimHistoryForFinish at Finish, trimHistory at save, a linear cap at load, and a cap inside backup restore — and clean #11 silently evicted an older clean (oldest non-full first); worse, an interrupted finish at the cap could lose the evicted clean for good even though the finish itself rolled back (audit RP-03/RP-01). All four points are retired together: appendHistory joins without dropping, save/load/restore pass history whole. No format change, no migration. The 1.152 NV crafted-oversize protection is re-homed, not lost: the quadratic trim it defended against no longer exists, every remaining history consumer is linear, and an oversize planted value is bounded by the storage quota and the restore's verified write set. (B2) PACE CORRECTNESS: a clean archived from this build carries the figures Finish itself measured — workM (the side-metres stamped INSIDE the run's own window, the live rate's stamp attribution since 1.93, so work banked before Start no longer inflates a saved clean's pace — RP-05), totalWorkM, and geoFP, a fingerprint of the route geometry as cleaned (RP-06: a later geometry edit can no longer silently re-price an archived clean — the stored figure is read verbatim, and the fingerprint makes any edit detectable). Mixed-shape entries can no longer undercount (RP-09). Pre-1.167 entries carry none of the new fields, derive exactly as before, and are never rewritten. (B3) HONEST DURATIONS, no math changed: the clean detail now shows BOTH intervals under truthful labels — "Streets done in" (start to last completion, the figure formerly mislabelled as the whole story) and "Total elapsed" (start to the Finish tap) — with the pace stated as work ÷ total elapsed, the window the rate actually divides by (RP-08: the two intervals differed by 15.8% on a 30-minute finish tail). A street whose sides were all marked at one moment reads "marked in one go" instead of inheriting the previous street's gap (RP-07); the stamp-less fallback is relabelled "Gap since previous street"; a skewed import can no longer render a negative duration. (B4) LEGACY CLEANS ARE VISIBLE: a timestamp-less legacy clean (every item time null) now appears in Previous cleans — "time not recorded" — opens, and deletes through the same two-tap flow; through 1.166 it was invisible yet could still be picked as the pace reference (RP-04). Reference selection is otherwise unchanged. (B5) TRAIL-CAP HONESTY: run distance is now accumulated fix-by-fix as it is recorded (a persisted counter, LS.rundist, resumed across a mid-run reload), so the archived km is the run's true distance even when the 8,000-point trail cap truncates the saved path; when the cap bit, the entry carries pathShortened and the clean detail says the saved GPS path was shortened (RP-02). A lost counter record re-derives from the surviving trail — the pre-1.167 figure, never worse. RIDE-ALONG: app1's stale "snap is never backed up" comment corrected (it has ridden backups in normalized form since 1.96/1.97). PROOF: the two history fixtures that used to PROVE the cap now prove retention (selftest-history) and the cap's absence (selftest-nv1152 NV4); drive177's premises re-derived (history retained whole, trail/session caps intact); NEW drives 186 (retention end-to-end — finish, reload, backup, restore at 12 cleans with zero evictions; an interrupted finish at the old capacity loses nothing; the B2 stored-figure preference, legacy fallback, and geometry-swap immunity) and 187 (both duration labels, zero-span wording, the negative guard, the listed-and-deletable legacy clean, and a >8,000-point run archiving its true distance with the flag). Storage shapes: docs/STORAGE-SCHEMA.md amended (uncapped history, the new entry fields, the v5.rundist record).

## Beta 1.166 — 2026-11-10
STORAGE FIX ROUND (Andrew's order, 2026-11-10, out of the same morning's storage audit: "let's not focus too much on the cloud" — two fixes, no cloud anything). (1) BACKUPS NOW CARRY THE SETTINGS: the v2 envelope gains a top-level `settings` field holding the Settings record (vibration, chime, chime sound — app1's SETTINGS_KEY) verbatim, read with the same B04 strictness as every other export read (a failed read fails the whole export; the recovery export carries the live settings in saveSettings' exact shape). The field is ADDITIVE, like nightExp before it: older builds ignore it in a new backup, and backups written before 1.166 simply omit it — their restores leave the device's toggles untouched (the missing-key rule). A restore validates the field through canonicalSettings (loadSettings' own sanitize rules, re-serialized canonically; a non-record value refuses the whole restore before anything is written) and lands it inside the same verified write set, with SETTINGS_KEY in the rollback scope. (2) CREATED ROUTES LEAVE THE MONOLITH: through 1.165 every created route's geometry lived in ONE localStorage record (snowroute.v2.userpacks) sharing the ~5 MB localStorage budget — one legal pack alone can be 1–4 MB, a failed save of the record endangered every route in it, and a corrupt record failed strict writers for unrelated routes. Now each route is its own record in a new IndexedDB database (snowroute-userdata, store `packs`, keyed by route id — a SIBLING of the city cache, which docs/STORAGE-SCHEMA.md contracts as re-downloadable-only) with one transaction per save and every write VERIFIED by read-back before its caller proceeds (upPut/upDel in parts/create-packs.js). MIGRATION on first 1.166 launch: the legacy record still registers synchronously at boot exactly as before (the app is usable on legacy data immediately and the record stays the fallback read); the async drain then copies every legacy route into the store, each write verified, parity is re-checked against a fresh full read, and only then is the marker (snowroute.v2.userpacks.migrated) written — itself verified — and the legacy record deleted (a failed delete is inert: the record drains again next boot against a complete store). A failed or interrupted drain leaves the app fully usable on the legacy data and retries next launch; boot never depends on a created-route save again, and one route's failed save refuses that route only (disclosed by the save's own toast). ALL readers/writers moved: saveDraftRoute is a single verified store put (the DA-05 whole-record read-modify-write hazard class is gone by construction); deleteRoute is a verified store delete plus removal of any not-yet-drained legacy shadow copy (a shadow-removal failure puts the store entry back); the backup export reads the verified in-memory picture of both homes (userPackCache — updated only at verified commit points; a boot whose store read failed fails the strict export, and the recovery export tops the cache up from validated memory exactly as DA-09 prescribed); a backup RESTORE snapshots the store values it will overwrite STRICTLY before writing (a route that cannot be read refuses the restore pre-write), lands the definitions as per-route verified puts FIRST, rolls the store back alongside localStorage on any later failure, and strips restored ids from any surviving legacy record inside the same verified write set — backups from older builds (geometry in the envelope) restore into the new store identically. localStorage holds zero created-route geometry after migration (the retained legacy fallback until verified, excepted). New drives 183 (migration: seeded monolith incl. one large route, marker semantics, interrupted-drain retry), 184 (the backup round-trip: full seeded state — routes, progress, created routes, settings, theme — export, wipe, restore, field-for-field; plus a pre-1.166 backup restoring into 1.166), 185 (per-route save isolation: a fault-injected save refuses only its own route; a corrupt stored record is skipped at boot while its siblings load; deleting one route leaves the others byte-identical); drives 150/154/156/163/165/166 and e2e-offline re-seamed to the store with their claims intact. Gated at the data-layer tier the plan job assigns (Tier C / full battery). Storage shapes: docs/STORAGE-SCHEMA.md amended (the userdata store, the migration marker, the settings envelope field).

## Beta 1.165 — 2026-11-10
THE BUTTONS WAIT FOR THE RISE (Andrew, on his 1.164 phone test, 2026-11-10: as the icon and the text animate up to the menu, the Create route / Choose route pills fade in WHILE the block is still rising, so it crosses them for about a second — "I'd rather have it after the icon and the text clear the path of the two options, then we fade those options in at that moment"). SHIPPED WITHOUT THE BATTERY at his explicit order the same morning ("Push that without testing") — a one-off override of the gating doctrine for this change, recorded here and in the round's ship record; the behaviour was verified by direct measurement on the assembled page (the round's MEASUREMENT.md), not by the drive battery. THE MECHANISM (parts/head.html + parts/create-save.js, nothing else): through 1.164 the boot panes' fade was the CSS rule #routeMenu.boot.show .rmPane — a .5s fade with a .55s delay, fired by arm() adding .show in the same instant it started the block's 1.3s flight, so the pills faded mid-flight underneath the travelling block. Now arm() puts a .hold class on #rmMain in that same synchronous block (head.html pins the held pane to its pre-show values against the .show fade), and a requestAnimationFrame watch samples the flight: the first frame the block's bottom edge is at or above the Choose pill's top edge in its landed position (the held pane sits 14px low; the pill's live top is discounted by that 14px), the hold releases and a .go class restarts THE PANE'S OWN FADE — the same .5s ease and .5s rise, its delay zeroed — at that moment. HARD FALLBACKS, because a menu can never be left without its buttons: the block's transitionend releases the hold too, and a 1500ms timer releases it unconditionally; first release wins, the release is idempotent, and finish() strips both classes so a pick can never strand them. THE GATE IS COLD-OPEN ONLY: reduced motion never arms it (no flight — the panes simply appear, as before), and returning to the main pane later (Back from Choose/Create) shows the pills immediately as always — there is no flight to clear. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: the entrance choreography, the flight (1.3s, same easing, same calc(35.49% - 209.42px) pose), the landed geometry, every other pane's fade and the 1.161 snow engine are byte-identical. Editorial: this entry; What's New rolled to the single Beta 1.165 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.165. fix-checks.json untouched (its roll follows separately); drive180's pane-fade timing pins still describe the 1.164 behaviour for that roll to re-derive.

## Beta 1.164 — 2026-11-10
THE LANDING DROPS BY HALF THE GAP (Andrew's rule, stated on his 1.163 phone test, 2026-11-10: "measure from the text — the bottom of SNOW ROUTE — to the top of the first button that says Choose route; whatever that distance is, divide it in half, and bring it lower that much"). SHIPPED WITHOUT THE BATTERY at his explicit order the same morning ("push this without any battery tests. No tests at all. Just push it.") — a one-off override of the gating doctrine for this change, recorded here and in the round's ship record; the geometry below was verified by direct measurement on the assembled page, which his instruction itself demanded, not by the drive battery. THE POSE (parts/head.html, the #brandLayer.menu > #brandBlock top): the 1.163 gap (landed title bottom to the Choose route pill's top) measured on the assembled page is affine in the screen height — 0.29H − 181, i.e. 33.61px at 360x740, 63.77 at 390x844, 80.00 at 420x900, 89.28 at 430x932, 115.97 at 768x1024 and 161.20 at 820x1180 — because the button's top sits at 0.5H − 64 and the block's top at 0.21H − 119 at every measured size, tablet included (the round's MEASUREMENT.md carries the table). The halving drop is therefore gap/2 = 0.1449H − 90.42, and the pose solves from calc(21% - 119px) to calc(35.49% - 209.42px): the new gap is half the old at both reference sizes (within ~0.01px) and the ratio holds at 0.500 across the whole sweep, so the landing stays proportional to the device. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: the splash start, the restored entrance, the buttons' positions and the 1.161 snow engine are byte-identical; drive180's SLOT/CLEAR pins still describe the 1.163 landing and are the registry follow-up round's to re-derive, per its notice. Editorial: this entry; What's New rolled to the single Beta 1.164 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.164.

## Beta 1.163 — 2026-11-10
THE ORIGINAL ENTRANCE, RESTORED + THE LANDING DROPS 24PX (Andrew's correction of 1.162 after his phone test, and his landing-samples pick, both 2026-11-10). What he said about 1.162: the combined rise itself was fine — "it looks good, the transition looks fine" — but the entrance was wrong: 1.162 had merged the icon and the title into one shared fade, and he wanted "the original animation where the icon fades in, then the text snow route slides in like the original, and then they both move up together". THE ENTRANCE (parts/head.html): the through-1.161 choreography is restored on the children inside #brandBlock — the icon carries its own fade again (#splashIcon: opacity 0, splashIconIn .35s ease at 1.25s, the Beta 1.160 placement) and the title carries its own slide again (#splashTitle: splashSlide .65s cubic-bezier(.16,.84,.3,1) at 1.5s, the Option 3 arrival) — both rules byte-for-byte their 1.161 forms apart from the flex child property the block requires, and the title's text stays SNOW ROUTE (1.162's Option B capitals; size, weight and letter-spacing unchanged). #brandBlock itself now carries NO animation (1.162's brandBlockIn keyframes are retired with its rule's animation): the block is purely the rise unit it was built to be — one rigid 236px column (the icon's 168 + the 26px gap + the title's 42px line), never reparented, and arm() (parts/create-save.js, comment-only touch — its code is byte-identical to 1.162, comments stripped and compared) still FLIPs THE BLOCK at the unchanged 2700ms floor over the same 1.3s cubic-bezier(.65,0,.35,1), so at the flip the pair travel as one piece and the icon never fades out. By the flip both entrances are long settled (the icon's fade completes at 1.6s, the slide's opacity at ~1.85s and its transform at 2.15s) and the slide's forwards fill is identity (opacity 1, translateX(0)), so the flight is the block's alone, exactly as 1.162 measured it. REDUCED MOTION: the media query returns to the through-1.161 semantics on the children (both static, opacity 1, animations off) and arm() lands the block's menu pose with no rise — the static block in the landed pose, icon above SNOW ROUTE (measured identical to the animated landing). THE LANDING (parts/head.html, the #brandLayer.menu > #brandBlock pose): Andrew's second correction was about the axis — the first samples round had lowered the START, and he corrected it himself: "I didn't mean the animation has to start lower... when it animates upward the final position for the next menu is too high up, I want it closer." The landing-height samples (today's / 24 / 48 / 72px lower, the buttons fixed in all four) put the choice in front of him; he picked Option B, 24px lower. The pose top moves calc(21% - 143px) -> calc(21% - 119px): the title still rides 194px down the block, and its landed centre moves from the 21% line + 72 to the 21% line + 96. Measured on the assembled page (the round's MEASUREMENT.md, the drives' own harness): landed title centre 285.00 at 420x900 and 273.23 at 390x844 against the slot's 285 / 273.24 (off by 0.00 / 0.01px), i.e. exactly 24px below the old landing; the CLEAR safeguard re-measured at the lower landing — the landed title clears the Choose route pill by 80.00px (420x900) and 63.77px (390x844), positive with room; the landed block's top clearance grows to 70px / 58.23px (from 1.162's 46 / 34.23); and the splash HOLD boxes are 1.162's own at both sizes (icon y332 / title y526 at 420x900) — the start does not move. FROZEN: the splash start, the Option 3 snow-only window and 2700ms floor, the plain-blue launch stills, the 1.161 snow engine entire (falling and accumulation), the caps title text and type, the title's main-pane-only lifetime, the buttons' positions, and every other behaviour. PROOF: drive180 restores the 1.161 SEQ arrival expectations (SEQ2 judges the icon's own fade again — fully in from 1.7s; SEQ3's hold window stands with its justification restored to the slide; S8's justification returns to the separate arrivals) and keeps BLOCK1-3 measuring the RISE phase unweakened (gap 26 +/- 2px, both opacities >= .99, centre-x drift <= 2px); SLOT re-pins to the new centre (+96) and CLEAR re-states the safeguard at the lower landing; drive132's S1 rationale re-pins in the same release (the fade is the icon's own again; its effective-opacity read and its assertion are unchanged). Editorial: this entry; What's New rolled to the single Beta 1.163 entry; the #ver label reads Beta 1.163; battery counts derive via scripts/release.py.

## Beta 1.162 — 2026-11-10
ONE BLOCK (Andrew's design, 2026-11-10, from the title-caps samples: he picked Option B — the title in capitals only — and directed the splash restructure in the same message: the icon and the title are one thing now). THE TITLE: the brand title becomes SNOW ROUTE (parts/head.html, the #splashTitle element only) — capitals only; its size (37px), weight (700) and letter-spacing (.2px) are byte-identical to the title-case rule it replaces, and every other name in the app (the settings app name, the tracker header) is untouched. THE BLOCK: through 1.161 the brand lived in two mirrored columns — the icon (with an invisible mirror of the title beneath it) inside #splash, the title in #brandLayer over an invisible spacer of the icon's box — and at the flip the icon faded out over .3s while the title rose alone. #splashBrand, #brandMirror and #brandSpacer are retired; the icon moves into #brandLayer as the title's sibling inside the new #brandBlock, a rigid 236px column (the icon's 168 + the 26px gap + the title's 42px line — the column as it has stood since 1.157, so the splash hold's boxes are unchanged: measured at 420x900 the icon still sits at y332 and the title at y526). The block carries ONE fade-in (.35s at 1.25s — Option 3's icon arrival, now shared; the title's separate 1.5s slide-in is retired with it) and arm() (parts/create-save.js) FLIPs THE BLOCK — one element, one transform, the same 1.3s cubic-bezier(.65,0,.35,1) — to a menu pose (top calc(21% - 143px)) whose arithmetic keeps the title's landed centre EXACTLY on the 1.159 slot (the title rides 194px down the block: block top = slot top - 194), so the pills and everything below the title never move. Measured on the assembled page (the round's MEASUREMENT.md): landed title centre 261.0 at 420x900 and 249.23 at 390x844 against the slot's 261 / 249.24; the landed block's top edge stands 46px (420x900) and 34.23px (390x844) below the screen top — clear of the 12px floor the round set — so the whole block shows in the menu, icon above SNOW ROUTE, as in Andrew's samples. NO FADE-OUT ANYWHERE: the icon stays at full opacity through the rise and in the menu; on a boot pick the landed block fades with the menu via the layer's existing .bye, and finish()/park hide the layer as before. REDUCED MOTION: the block is static from the first frame and arm() lands its menu pose with no rise — the icon stays in the menu there too (through 1.161 the reduced-motion query hid the brand at the flip, matching the animated fade; both retired together; measured landed pose identical); the 1900ms floor is unchanged. FROZEN: the Option 3 opening (the snow-only window, the 2700ms arm() floor), the plain-blue launch stills, the 1.161 snow engine entire (falling and accumulation), the +72 slot, the title's main-pane-only lifetime, and every other behaviour. PROOF: drive180 re-points its trace at the block (the transition hooks, the from-value, the rest box) and re-pins what the redesign retires — SEQ2 judges the shared arrival (icon AND title fully in from 1.7s), SEQ3's hold window stands, S2 covers both elements' parents, N4 judges the block — and OVERLAP is retired for three new checks that measure Andrew's stated requirement itself: BLOCK1 (the icon-to-title gap stays 26 +/- 2px on every flight sample, hold to settle), BLOCK2 (both effective opacities >= .99 on every sample from the flip to the settle — no fade either way), BLOCK3 (the block's horizontal centre drifts <= 2px across the flight — the rise is purely vertical). drive132 S1 re-pins (the SNOW ROUTE text; the icon's first-frame absence read as effective opacity through the block). SNOW1-SNOW9, SLOT and CLEAR are unweakened.

## Beta 1.161 \u2014 2026-11-10
STAGE E, ADOPTED (Andrew, 2026-11-10: on the snow-realism examples page he judged the Stage E sample \u2014 falling copied from a Getty/iStock snowfall video he supplied \u2014 "perfect", with one condition stated in the same breath: the snow must keep accumulating like the original, just higher up). THE FALLING is ported into parts/brand-snow.js from the sample's recipe: three layers sized by the scene's area, so the sample's density is what any screen sees \u2014 a dense field of tiny sharp specks (one per ~600 px\u00b2, radius .5\u20131.1 px, fall 14\u201330 px/s, a gentle sway and a per-speck brightness twinkle), a mid layer of soft flakes (one per ~4400 px\u00b2, radius 1.8\u20133.4 px, fall 26\u201348 px/s) drawn from a radial-gradient sprite rendered once at startup, and a few rare, very large, extra-soft near blobs (one per ~45000 px\u00b2, capped 4\u20138, radius 26\u201352 px, fall 34\u201356 px/s \u2014 slightly faster than the specks) drawn from a second pre-rendered sprite at low alpha: out-of-focus flakes passing near the lens, which is why they fall through the ground plane and out of the frame rather than landing on it. Slow, calm, dreamy \u2014 no gusts, no bursts; the video's dark backdrop and warm glow were its lighting and are not carried over (the app stays flat brand blue). The frame loop only draws \u2014 every soft edge is pre-rendered, never a per-frame filter. THE ACCUMULATION obeys the condition literally: the 16 s build/rest/melt cycle, the two-sine ground profile and the paint are byte-identical to Beta 1.158\u20131.160; the ONLY change is the peak value, 20 \u2192 58 px \u2014 58 being the rendered height of the app's bottom bar (#tabBar: Locate / Routes / Home / Clear / Settings), measured in the assembled page at 390\u00d7844 and identically at 420\u00d7900 (6 px top padding + a 46 px tab item + 6 px, the safe-area inset reading 0 in emulation), so the finished snow band stands about as tall as that bar. Frozen with it: the Option 3 opening (snow-only window, icon 1.25 s, title 1.5 s, flip 2.7 s), the single canvas, the title's main-pane-only lifetime, the +72 landing, the plain launch stills, and reduced-motion behaviour (the engine still never starts there). TESTS: drive180 gains SNOW9, the peak proof \u2014 at the settle cycle's full amount it reads the painted crest off the canvas (the 99th-percentile column height of the unbroken near-white run from the canvas floor, the lower of two reads 700 ms apart, so a flake caught landing cannot fake it) and demands it within 5 px of the expected crest (the pinned 58 px peak \u00d7 the ground profile's maximum over the live width), with the bar itself measured live in the same page at 58 \u00b1 2 px. SNOW1\u2013SNOW8 stand unweakened; SNOW5's stated justification is updated to the new field (worst lateral sway 6.6 px/s per flake, still under its 7.6 px/s claim; respawn centroid steps shrink with the far larger particle count). Battery counts derive via scripts/release.py.

## Beta 1.160 — 2026-11-09
OPTION 3 ADOPTED (Andrew's design — device-proven the same evening on the temporary Snow Probe, then adopted on his verdict, with his one condition recorded below). The icon glitch that survived the November ghost hunt — the splash icon doubling or hopping inside iOS's launch handover on some cold opens — is attacked by removing the icon from the handover entirely, from BOTH sides. (1) THE LAUNCH STILLS ARE PLAIN BLUE: the twelve apple-touch-startup-image files are now solid splash blue #2b4bf2, no icon and no title (gen_launch.py gains a plain mode and stays the single source; the filenames and the link tags are unchanged). A still cannot show falling snow, so plain blue is the honest match for the live scene's first frame. (2) THE OPENING RETIMES to the probe's proven sequence: the app opens on blue + falling snow alone — the icon and the title are in the layout from the first frame (their boxes reserved, so nothing shifts when they arrive) but not painted; the icon fades in at 1.25 s (a .35 s fade on #splashIcon itself in parts/head.html — deliberately not on #splashBrand, whose opacity is the flip's fade-out and would be pinned visible by a filling animation); the title slides in at 1.5 s (its splashSlide delay moves 1 s to 1.5 s); and the boot gate's splash floor moves 1900 to 2700 ms in arm() (parts/create-save.js) so the flip lands at the probe's 2.7 s, after the hold. The icon then fades out with the brand as the title rises 1.3 s to the same +72 landing, pills fading in beneath. Everything else is untouched: the snow engine, its single canvas, its parameters and lifetime; the landing slot; the title's main-menu-only lifetime; and every viewport defence. Under reduced motion nothing animates — icon and title show statically and the gate keeps its 1900 ms floor, exactly as through 1.159. This is NOT the reverted Beta 1.84 experiment: 1.84 blanked the LIVE side while the still still carried the icon, so the icon vanished for about 0.87 s at the handover — the mismatch was the artifact. Option 3 removes the icon from both frames; there is nothing to vanish or to double. ANDREW'S CONDITION: on the probe he sometimes saw the snow stopping short of the screen bottom, a blue gap under the snow's ground line (the probe page carried none of the app's viewport defences). This release imports ONLY the timing and the stills — the snow engine, its canvas, its container and the viewport defences stay exactly as production has them — and drive180 gains two standing guards for that failure property itself: SNOW7 (the canvas's box covers the full viewport height at every traced frame, its bottom within 2 px of the viewport height) and SNOW8 (the accumulation's painted band sits at the canvas's bottom edge). drive180 also re-pins the sequence: SEQ1 (neither icon nor title is painted before 1.2 s), SEQ2 (the icon fully in after its fade), SEQ3 (the title fully in before the flip) and S8 (the flip at the 2.7 s floor). THE PROBE IS DELETED in this same release: probe-f.html and the twelve launch-o3-*.png stills are removed — the trial is over and the verdict is recorded; the commit history keeps them recoverable (the November precedent).

## Beta 1.159 — 2026-11-09
Four items from Andrew's phone test of Beta 1.158, shipped as one release. ITEM 1 — TITLE LIFETIME (Andrew's rule): the "Snow Route" title appears ONLY in the main menu (the cold-open Choose a route / Create new route pane). Once either is chosen, the title is gone from every following menu — the route list, the create doors, the city list, the name-it pane — and it returns when navigation comes back to the main pane. The mechanism is one hook in the one place all pane changes pass through: showRmPane (parts/app2.js) carries an .away class on the title's #brandLayer for every pane except rmMain, and the layer fades at the menu's own .3s shape (a new base transition + the .away rule in parts/head.html). The title is never reparented and its pose machinery (.menu/.bye/.off/.parked, the FLIP) is untouched — .away only rides alongside; in the tab-mode menu the layer is parked and the toggle is inert. ITEM 2 — CITY PILLS CENTRED (Andrew): the blue menus' list rows — the city list (Select City), the manual-search results and the choose-route list, every one a .routeRow inside #routeMenu — now carry CENTRED text and the unified pills' full font treatment, closing the gap 1.158 left (the rows had taken the tint and ring but kept their left alignment and their smaller name). In parts/head.html: the row's .routeMeta is centred on the pill itself (spanning the row's full width, vertically centred, side padding keeping wrapped lines clear of the right-edge extras), the name takes the .rmBtn pill's 19 px (its weight was already the pill's 650), and the sub line takes the pill sub-line's 500 weight (its 13 px and .75 ink already matched). The chevron, the Currently loaded badge and a created route's Delete stay in flow at the right edge; row content, order and behaviour are unchanged. ITEM 3 — SNOW TWITCH (Andrew's directed fix): opening Beta 1.158, he felt the falling snow twitch sideways at the exact moment the splash hands over to the menu — the same instant every cold open, "like clockwork". Root cause: 1.158 drew the scene on TWO canvases, one in the splash and one inside #routeMenu, and the menu's boot entrance rides a translateX(-26 px) slide home plus an opacity ramp, so the menu-half snow field physically slid sideways while fading in over the static splash-half field — two fields trading places at the handoff. The fix is his design, the same pattern that fixed the title at 1.155: ONE continuous layer. The #snowCvM canvas is deleted from the menu; the single canvas is #snowCv inside #splash (a top-level fixed layer spanning the whole opening scene, never reparented, never transformed), and while the scene is live the engine carries a body.snow-scene class under which the boot menu's background is transparent, so the one field paints through under the menu's panes — behind the title and the pills in both halves of the scene (a lone fixed canvas cannot sit between the menu's background and its panes from outside; they are one stacking context, which is why the canvas's home is the splash layer and the menu's blue steps aside instead). The engine's parameters are byte-identical to 1.158 (85 flakes, radius 1.1–3.6 px, fall 34–72 px/s, sway 7 px at 0.7, the 16 s ground build/rest/melt cycle, the two-sine ground profile to 20 px); a resize now preserves flake positions proportionally instead of re-scattering them; the test handle's flake x is the rendered position (base + sway). Lifetime otherwise follows 1.158's scene-end semantics (stops for good at the pick; pauses hidden; never starts under reduced motion), with one addition that rides Item 1: the snow hides in the menu's sub-panes alongside the title and returns at the main pane. When the scene ends for good, stop() removes body.snow-scene, so the tab menu, the delete-return picker and reduced-motion boots keep the menu's own opaque blue. ITEM 4 — LANDING +72 (Andrew's tap decision): the title's menu landing moves from 1.158's +48 to the examples page's last option, +72 — one value in parts/head.html (the menu pose top calc(21% + 27px) → calc(21% + 51px)); the buttons do not move. At the drive viewport (420×900) the landed centre moves from 237 px to 261 px. The safeguard he was told stands in the test: drive180's CLEAR check must still measure a positive margin between the landed title's bottom and the first button's top, or the ship stops. TESTS: drive180 gains TITLE1–TITLE5 (title lifetime across the pane round trips, effective opacity), SNOW4 (one canvas, one parent, no rect drift across every trace sample), SNOW5 (the painted field's pixel centroid never jumps sideways across the handoff), SNOW6 (snow hides/returns with the title), ROWC/ROWC2/ROWF (centred, pill-typed rows — the choose list's real rows and a city row from the app's own routeRowShell factory), re-pins SLOT to +72 and retires SNOW1's two-canvas premise; the round ran tests-first: the new checks were committed against the 1.158 app and ran RED there (SLOT off by exactly the 24 px step, the two-canvas count, no .away anywhere, left-aligned rows) before the fix was committed. Gate: the diff touches deep paths (tests/, parts/create-save.js), so the new tier map selects Tier C — the full battery plus offline-e2e. Battery counts derive via scripts/release.py.

## Beta 1.158 — 2026-11-09
Title landing height + blue-menu pill unification + falling snow (three Andrew decisions — the first two from the landing-height examples page, the snow from the falling-snow examples page — shipped as ONE release at his directive — "forget about beta 1.159 and put the pill styling in with 1.158"). PART 1 — LANDING (Andrew picked "+48 px lower" from Current / +24 / +48 / +72): the splash title's LANDING position in the route menu moves 48 px DOWN from the Beta 1.157 slot; everything else about the transition is frozen. The change is a single CSS value in parts/head.html — the menu pose of #brandLayer's title: `top: calc(21% - 21px)` → `calc(21% + 27px)`, the -21 px half-line offset plus Andrew's 48 — so the landed centre moves from the 21% line to the 21% line + 48 px. The slot's definition is single (sweep-verified: "21%" occurs nowhere else in parts/ as a landing definition; the copies in index.html and src/tracker-source.html are build outputs; arm()'s FLIP measures live boxes and carries no constant). The buttons do NOT move. The splash pose (26 px icon-to-title gap), the 37 px size throughout, the 1.3 s rise, the icon's .3 s fade lead, start-frame identity — every 1.155/1.157 guarantee — stand untouched. Geometry at the drive viewport (420x900): landed box y168..210 (centre 189) → y216..258 (centre 237), exactly 48 px. PART 2 — PILLS (Andrew, on the examples page's route-menu mock: he liked the centred text, the stronger white outline around the pill, the tinted faded fill, and that "Choose route is solid and Create new route is not — that's a good effect"; directed into ALL the blue menus): the mock's actual values, extracted from the artifact's built source (its .menu-btn / .menu-btn.secondary) — primary SOLID white fill, deep-blue ink #173a8a, drop shadow 0 6px 18px rgba(0,0,0,.18), no ring; secondary tinted fill rgba(255,255,255,.16), white ink, the outline as an inset ring `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1.5px rgba(255,255,255,.75)`; radius 14 px; text centred — now drive THE one shared pill definition, .rmBtn in parts/head.html, which both blue menus (#routeMenu and #setMenu) already share: the default .rmBtn IS the secondary, a new .rmBtn.primary is the solid, and .rmBtn.quiet stays the secondary's quiet modifier (lighter tint .07, fainter ring .35 — the photo door's Beta 1.124 tier). The mock's 17 px type / 17 px padding were its 360 px stage's scale; the app's 19 px menu type and 18 px padding stay. Primary opt-ins (the one dominant action per pane; full inventory + calls in the ship record): Choose a route, Select from list (first create door; Search manually is secondary, the photo door quiet), Choose photo, Import my draft, the last-city shortcut, Save route, Update, Backup (JSON), Download (offline maps). Settings' top-level rows (Status / On the job / Backup / About) are navigation peers — all secondary; Delete maps stays secondary (destructive actions do not wear the primary solid). Family tokens, layout unchanged: the menus' list rows (#routeMenu .routeRow — name left, badge/chevron right) and the Settings card rows (.m-card — labelled toggle/nav rows) take the same tint + ring; centring is for action pills, and Andrew's "if possible" governs the rows. Examined and OUT: .rmBack text links (Andrew's Beta 1.127 text-link design, not pills), .rmInput fields, the modal row buttons and banner buttons (glass card surfaces, not blue menus), the create sheets' .crPrimary/.crGhost/.crDanger (card-solid sheet system), #crHintBtn (a solid centred pill already, floating over the map), the search/dock/tab/run controls (not menu pills). Proof: drive180 gains seven checks (22 total). SLOT pins the settled centre-y to 21% of the viewport height + 48 within 2 px; against the 1.157 slot it fails by exactly the design delta — the RED is arithmetic from the recorded 1.157 rule (top calc(21% - 21px) over the 42 px line centres the title on the 21% line itself, 48 px above the new expectation). CLEAR measures the landed title's bottom edge against the first button's (Choose route) top edge and demands a positive margin — the examples page showed +72 crowding the buttons and +48 picked to clear; the check carries the measured margin in its name. PILLC measures the boot-menu pills' label centring on the RENDERED TEXT (a Range over each button's contents vs the button's centre; tolerance 2 px — not a computed-style proxy), and PILLF the computed fills (primary exactly rgb(255,255,255); secondary exactly rgba(255,255,255,0.16) with the 1.5 px inset ring in its shadow). The drive updates are TEST changes driven by Andrew's design decisions, logged as such; no other drive asserts the old button styling (swept: no drive reads these buttons' text alignment, radius or fills). Gates (light lane, Andrew's current efficiency directive): build-pages --check + tsc, and one branch CI run whose merge gate is the build job plus drive180; other drives' results are recorded in the ship record for the nightly 1 AM full battery. No storage shape changes. Battery counts derive via scripts/release.py. PART 3 — SNOW (Andrew, later the same evening: reviewed the falling-snow examples page, picked "Falls and settles", and directed it into this same release before the merge): a snowfall layer for the boot/menu scene only, built from the examples artifact's built source — its "settles" configuration verbatim: 85 flakes, radius 1.1–3.6 px, fall speed 34–72 px/s, sway amplitude 7 px at sway speed 0.7 (per-flake frequency ×0.7–1.3, amplitude ×0.5–1.2), flake alpha .9 × per-flake .55–1 with a .6 s fade-in; the accumulation runs the mock's 16 s cycle (builds ~9 s ease-out, rests 3 s, melts 3 s, empty 1 s) as a ground profile up to 20 px high shaped by 0.72 + 0.16 sin(x·.045 + 1.7) + 0.12 sin(x·.013 + .4), filled with the mock's white gradient at alpha min(1, amount × 1.4); device pixel ratio capped at 1.5, frame dt clamped at .05 s. Two app adaptations, stated: flakes respawn at the TOP and fall the full height of the scene (the mock respawned them in a band around the title — a replay-framing device), and ONE engine (parts/create-save.js) drives TWO canvases from the same state — #snowCv inside #splash (over its blue, under the brand) for the splash hold, #snowCvM as #routeMenu's first child (over its blue, under the panes — #routeMenu .rmPane gains position:relative so the panes paint above it) for the menu — so the snow sits behind the title (the brand layer) and behind the pills in both halves of the scene and never covers text or controls. Lifetime mirrors the brand layer's: starts at boot, pauses while the page is hidden, stops for good when the boot scene ends (the pick fade's .bye / the layer's .off / the menu parked), never restarts for the tab-mode menu, and never starts under reduced motion (the standing convention — the same media query arm() branches on). drive180 gains SNOW1–SNOW3 (canvases present in both halves + engine running; flake positions change across frames sampled 650 ms apart; accumulation amount and ground height > 0 in the build phase) — state read through the engine's window.__snow handle, no pixel checks.

## Beta 1.157 \u2014 2026-11-09
Road-tap width narrowing (Andrew's device feedback, 2026-11-09: when selecting roads he could tap "pretty far to the right or left of a road" and still select it — the touch width is narrowed, still comfortably tappable, more precise; scope confirmed by Andrew for BOTH the road pick and sector trimming). THE CHANGE (parts/create-pick.js only): the two tap handlers' box halves — crMapTap (road picking) and crSectorTap (sector trimming) both looped `for (const half of [16, 28])` over a square query box around the tap, first box naming any road deciding, closest line inside the box winning — are now ONE shared literal, `const CR_TAP_BOXES = [12, 20]`, read by both loops. The discipline is otherwise identical (square box, first-non-empty-box decides, closest wins, name-is-identity), and the two-step shape Beta 1.119 set after the opposite device failure (point queries hitting nothing, "No road there" on every tap) is kept — only the widths narrow. LEAK AUDIT, so the narrowing is real end-to-end: (a) crSectorTap's geometry fallback — the nearest part of the SAME street when the rendered query answers nothing (the realistic case: tiles not yet loaded after a camera move) — was capped only at 75 METRES (~87 px at the pick zoom 17, ~44 px at zoom 16), far looser than any box: a far tap could toggle a sector through the back door. It now judges distance in the same projected pixels as the boxes (new helper crFeatDistPx beside crFeatDistM), takes the nearest part by pixel distance, and refuses beyond the retry half (CR_TAP_BOXES[1], 20 px); the 75 m rule stays as an absolute guard on top (it binds first when zoomed far out, where 20 px spans more than 75 m), and without a projection the metre-only rule stands. (b) The invisible crDraftHit layer's painted width (24 px) DOES add reach on the sector path, stated openly: MapLibre GL JS 4.7.1's LineStyleLayer.queryIntersectsFeature tests the query box against the line geometry buffered by half the painted width, so the sector path's true reach is box half + 12 px — [28, 40] px through 1.155, [24, 32] px now. The width itself is unchanged (it is the draft lines' own tap-target size from Beta 1.127, outside this change); because it is a constant +12 on both steps, the box change narrows each step by exactly the box delta (4 px first, 8 px retry). On the pick path the basemap's own road paint widths add their half-widths the same way — the box remains the app's controlled term. (c) A sweep of the whole create flow found no other tap/selection tolerance: crResolve (create-citydata.js) picks the nearest RUN of the street the box already named — it cannot name a road, so it is not a leak (examined, left). The on-the-job road tap (app2.js, a different mechanism — nearestStreet in projected px, `near.d <= 16`) is OUT OF SCOPE and deliberately untouched. Proof: new drive182 grades the geometry honestly at the map seam — its queryRenderedFeatures stub is BOX-AWARE (it emulates the library's own rule — segment vs box buffered by half the layer's painted width, 0 for the fixture basemap roads, 12 for crDraftHit — over the fixture extract's real geometry, sector features rebuilt from the app's own crStreetParts), and its far/retry probe taps are selected by SEGMENT-TO-SQUARE distance — the quantity the box query itself tests (see the corner correction below) — while the muted-fallback probes keep point distances, because the fallback is point-judged; the measured distances ride in the check names. 11 checks: a 10 px tap picks (first box), a tap missing the 12 square but hitting the 20 picks (retry), a tap missing the 20 square entirely picks NOTHING, a 10 px sector tap toggles, a tap beyond the new rendered reach toggles NOTHING, the same far tap with the renderer muted toggles NOTHING through the fallback, and a muted 15 px tap still toggles through it. RED on 1.155 (CI run 42714313780): 8/11 — exactly P3, S2 and S3 fail, each on the designed geometry (the 24 px tap picked Alderson inside the old 28 box; the 36 px tap toggled inside the old rendered reach 40, probe measured 36.0 px with the next sector 104.9 px away; the muted 36 px tap toggled a sector back on under the old 75 m cap). The RED took four attempts to earn, all three failures TEST-classified and fixed in the drive, never the app: attempt 1 crashed on an unguarded null probe before printing results (fireTap now null-safe); attempt 2's flow let an earlier probe's pick open a sector session that routed later pick taps to trimming (a tap on an already-picked street opens its sectors, Beta 1.128 — the probes now run P1, P3, P2 so each tap meets an unpicked street); attempt 3's S3 asserted the chosen set's LENGTH, which a toggle-off/toggle-on pair restores — S2/S3 now assert the chosen SET is unchanged. In the final RED run, shard 4's drive181 failed S2a/S2b (17/19) — the documented drive181 S2a scenario race (Beta 1.155's round recorded 3 greens : 1 fail on an identical tree; this branch's app code was untouched 1.155 and drive181 passed this round's other three runs): FLAKE, recorded, not re-run — the RED conclusion never rested on it. THE CORNER CORRECTION (combined-round diagnosis, 2026-11-09): on the FIXED app the drive then went 8/11 again (CI run 42715921528) — P3 and S2 failing the other way. The probes had been placed by POINT-to-line distance, but a box query tests segment against SQUARE: a line whose closest approach to the tap point is 24 px can still clip the ±20 square's corner zone (corner reach 20·√2 ≈ 28.3 px), and a line 36 px off can clip the sector path's buffered corner (28.3 + 12 ≈ 40.3 px) — the app was obeying its box discipline exactly, and the drive was grading a quantity the mechanism does not test (TEST class; S4 failed purely as S2's cascade, and P2's pass in that run was polluted by P3). The probes are now selected by segment-to-square distance (each far/retry probe misses the new square by a margin and hits the old one, so the RED above is deterministic, not geometric luck), and the foreign-writes census fixture is re-pinned for the one adjudicated site this change's added lines moved (create-pick.js followPaused, line 608 → 629; the site and the totals are unchanged). SPLASH REFINEMENT — the second half of this combined release (Andrew's phone test of 1.155, ~17:06 EST: the one-text fix works, but the title overlapped the icon; the distance between icon and text should be equivalent to before the animation update; the text must stay the same size throughout and just animate up; and slow it down a little, make it smooth). Measured first: through 1.152 the splash brand was ONE centred column (icon 168 + the title's 26 px margin + its 42 px line = 236), so at the drive viewport the icon sat at y332..500 with a 26 px gap to the title at y526. Beta 1.153 took the title out of the brand; from then on the splash centred the LONE icon (y366..534 — its centre fell 34 px) while the title's layer kept posing it at y526 — an 8 px OVERLAP in the splash hold itself, and drive180's 1.155 assertions never sampled the icon.
